# UltiTools-API — UAT Checklist

This document is the executable companion to `FEATURES.md`: one row per feature stating the
steps to exercise it and the observable truth that proves it works. It is an internal reference
for real-machine verification, not user-facing documentation.

> Batches are dispatched at 60 rows or fewer, and a batch never spans two repositories. There are
> exactly one standing exit to `human-uat-pending`: a row needing the pixel layer while the
> real-client harness is not ready. A row needing UltiCloud or panel credentials is run by the
> tester with the UAT account, not handed to the maintainer, and no row in this file uses Layer
> `human`. Beyond that, only a row the per-row triage assigns to the maintainer — because it needs
> the maintainer's own physical device or subjective human judgement, with that reason stated — may
> end `human-uat-pending`. Every other row must reach `pass`, `fail`, or `blocked`.

## Conventions

- **Columns:** `ID`, `Preconditions`, `Steps`, `Expected`, `Layer`, `Covers`.
- **ID:** cites its `FEATURES.md` ID verbatim. A negative case suffixes the checklist ID only,
  as `.neg-<slug>` — a negative case still tests the same feature, so the base ID is unchanged.
- **Layer**, copied verbatim from Laojun's own `ultitools-real-client-uat` skill so no
  translation step exists at dispatch time: `protocol`, `java-client`, `os-input`, `pixel`,
  `server`, `human`.
- **Unit regression controls:** `unit` marks repository tests, not real Paper UAT. A green unit row
  cannot discharge a `server` obligation; every configuration real-server obligation has its own row.
- **Delegated-authorization UltiCloud rows (D-27b, corrected by Phase 15 D-01, rewritten for protocol v2 in
  6.3.1):** a row whose Steps can only be exercised through an authenticated UltiCloud panel session carries the
  fixed Preconditions phrase `an authorized UltiCloud session under the maintainer's delegated authorization`
  (appended to, not replacing, the row's own preconditions) and Layer `server`. The maintainer delegates that
  authorization to the dispatching agent, which acts through the **staging test account** of the backend staging
  stack described below — never through the maintainer's own UltiCloud account, which no row logs in to, links or
  uses. A row whose Preconditions begin with the marker `` Env: `staging` `` (below) meets this convention through
  that marker and does not repeat the phrase. A row blocked because the staging stack or its test account is not
  available records that as its dispatch-time `blocked` status in the canonical verdict file, not as a property of
  this checklist's Layer column.
- **UltiCloud protocol v2 rows (6.3.1).** Every row of `## /ulticloud — cloud authentication` and
  `## UltiCloud connection — panel requests and delivery`, and every other row that reaches UltiCloud or its stub
  (the `/upm` stub rows, the panel capability rows, the log stream, metrics and remote surface guard rows, and the
  configuration rows that edit a file through the panel), begins its Preconditions with exactly one environment
  marker, and a row never mixes two environments. Each row also assumes `language: en` in
  `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` unless it says otherwise. The authority for which rows are such rows is the
  `CHECKLIST_IDS` list of `UltiTools-API`'s own automated check of them, `UltiCloudUatRowsTest`.
  - `` Env: `staging` `` — the backend staging stack (`https://api-staging.ultikits.com`, its gateway
    `wss://api-staging.ultikits.com/v1/agent` and its panel) and the **staging server root**, a Paper server
    running the staging build (handover item 1). The UltiCloud account is the **staging test account** the backend
    session provides for the run (its name starts with `uc-uat-`), never the maintainer's own account; every
    server it links is named `uc-uat-<run id>` on the confirmation page. Panel-side requests are sent by the backend
    session with its staging operations tool,
    `node scripts/staging-ops.mjs realtime <method> --server <server ID> --input '<JSON>'`. The tool and its `realtime` command exist on ulticloud commit `0b63cba` (branches
    `feature/p07-14` and `feature/p07-uat-triggers`), not on ulticloud `main`; the backend session runs them from a
    checkout of that commit with its staging ops token, which stays with that session — it is never copied into the
    run directory, a prompt, a log or a verdict file — and the run schedules its staging window with that session
    first. `<method>` is a Realtime RPC name of the tool's allow-list (`REALTIME_METHODS` in
    `scripts/staging-ops.mjs`), never a protocol frame type; the tool refuses any other name before it sends
    anything. The tool prints the response's `data`, which for `realtime` is
    `{"method":…,"serverId":…,"result":…}`, `result` being the RPC's return value
    (`apps/api/staging/ops/actions.ts`, `callRealtime`). A row names the method, its `--input` and the field of
    `result` it reads. In an input, `<C1>`, `<C2>`, … are fresh ULIDs (26 Crockford base-32 characters) the backend
    session generates, one per request, and `<RB>` is `{"kind":"system","label":"uc-uat-<run id>"}`. The RPCs that
    send to the plugin answer before the plugin does (`apps/realtime/src/rpc/contract.ts` at that commit):
    `enqueueCommand` and `requestPower` return `{"ok":true,"entry":{…}}` with the entry's `status` `delivered`, and
    the plugin's result is read afterwards with `listCommands` `{"limit":10}`, in the item whose `commandId` is the
    request's: its `status` becomes `succeeded` when the plugin's result is `ok` and `failed` otherwise, and its
    `output` is the plugin's `data.output` lines on success and `[]` on failure (`apps/realtime/src/hub/commands.ts`,
    `toEntry`), so a staging row observes only a command's success and its output lines. `requestOp` returns
    `{"ok":true,"opId":"<ULID>"}`, and the plugin's result is read with `getOp` `{"opId":"<that ULID>"}`, repeated
    while its `state` is `pending` (at most 30 seconds): `state` is then `succeeded`, `partial` or `failed`, `result`
    the plugin's data as the gateway maps it, and `error` the plugin's `{code, message}` on failure
    (`apps/realtime/src/hub/requests.ts`, `toLedger`). `requestModule` waits for the plugin and returns
    `{"ok":true,"outcome":…}`, the plugin's `module.response` payload unchanged. Gateway-side behaviour (the 10-minute expiry of queued
    commands, the 24-hour redelivery cap, a gateway-side `power_disabled`) is verified by the backend's own gateway
    tests, not by these rows.
  - `` Env: `stub` `` — the local stub gateway and catalogue (plan 22-19) and the **stub server root**, a second
    Paper server running the stub build (handover item 2). The stub server root never talks to staging and the
    staging server root never to the stub. Start the stub from a checkout of the handed-over commit, wrapping the
    Maven call in the machine's shared build lock: first
    `mvn -B -q -DskipTests test-compile dependency:build-classpath -Dmdep.outputFile=target/stub.classpath -Dmdep.includeScope=test`,
    then
    `java -cp "target/test-classes:target/classes:$(cat target/stub.classpath)" com.ultikits.ultitools.stub.UltiCloudStub --http-port 18080 --admin-port 18081 --access-log <run dir>/stub-access.log`.
    It prints `UltiCloudStub http  listening on 127.0.0.1:18080 …`, `UltiCloudStub admin listening on
    127.0.0.1:18081 …` and the access-log path; both ports bind 127.0.0.1 only. Stop it with `kill <pid>`
    (SIGTERM; a background job ignores Ctrl-C), which writes `STUB stopped` to the access log. The admin API
    takes JSON bodies on `http://127.0.0.1:18081`, and every POST writes one `ADMIN POST <path> <body> ->
    <status>` line: `GET /_stub` lists the endpoints; `POST /_stub/send` with `{"type":…,"payload":…}` (and
    optionally `"id"` or `"replyTo"`) sends one gateway-to-plugin frame built from its vendored fixture with the
    given payload keys replacing the fixture's, schema-checked first (an invalid frame is answered 400 and not
    sent; a missing `replyTo` names the newest request it answers); `GET /_stub/frames?type=<type>` returns the
    recorded frames the plugin sent (`&direction=out` the stub's own, `&direction=all` both); `GET /_stub/status` names the
    live connection and the credential fingerprints. A row names every other endpoint and body it posts. An
    access-log line reads `<ISO-8601 UTC instant> <CATEGORY> <text>`, with the categories `STUB`, `HTTP`,
    `HTTP-RESPONSE`, `V1`, `TCP-RESET`, `REST-VALID`, `REST-INVALID`, `UPGRADE`, `WS-OPEN`, `WS-IN`, `WS-OUT`,
    `WS-INVALID`, `WS-NOTE`, `WS-CLOSED`, `WS-ERROR`, `CREDENTIAL` and `ADMIN`; a credential appears only as
    `ucs_***#<first 8 hex of its SHA-256>`. The stub server root links to the stub with `/ulticloud login` from
    the console: the stub answers the first poll `authorization_pending` and approves at the second, so within about 15 seconds the console
    shows `UltiCloud: this server is now linked as <name> (server ID <ULID>).` and `UltiCloud: connected.`.
    "Linked to the stub" in a row means that has happened and `GET /_stub/status` shows one live connection;
    "connected" means `/ulticloud status` reads `Connection: connected`.
  - `` Env: `server` `` — no UltiCloud at all: the staging server root or the stub server root, used only through
    its console and its files; the row needs no network.
- **Handover items for the UltiCloud protocol v2 rows.** Each item is handed over with its path, version, byte
  size, SHA-256 and source commit; the SHA-256 is taken from a clean build after the final commit, and the
  executor fails closed on a mismatch.
  1. **Staging build** — the framework JAR built with `-Dultitools.api.url=https://api-staging.ultikits.com`.
  2. **Stub build** — the framework JAR of the same commit built with `-Dultitools.api.url=http://127.0.0.1:18080`;
     a separate JAR with its own SHA-256, never the staging build relabelled.
  3. **UltiLogin 2.1.0** — built against this framework (UltiLogin plans 01-02 and 01-03), for
     `ultitools.cloud.player-link` and `ultitools.cloud.player-link.redelivery-restart`.
  4. **UltiLogin 2.0.0** — the released JAR, for `ultitools.cloud.player-link.neg-ultilogin-2-0-0` only.
  5. **UAT probe module** — a test module built against this framework by the dispatching session (not part of
     this repository). Until it is in the handover, the rows that name it are `blocked`, not run. It has the
     identify-string `uatprobe`, `plugin.yml` `version: 1.0.0` and `api-version: 630`, and its enable hook:
     registers a panel responder under `uatprobe.echo` that answers the JSON object `{"echo": <params.text>}`
     (so its `hello` declares `modules:uatprobe@1.0.0`); registers the command name `uatsecret` with
     `UltiTools#getSensitiveCommandRegistry()`; registers a `@ConfigEntity("config/uatprobe.yml")` entity with two `@ConfigEntry` fields,
     the boolean `flag` and the string `label`, whose shipped file is the three lines `# uat probe settings`,
     `flag: false` and `label: probe`. Its
     commands, for any sender with no permission and no `requireOp`: `/uatsecret <text>` replies `uatsecret ok`;
     `/uatsecret fail <text>` throws `IllegalStateException` with the message
     `uatprobe failure on /uatsecret fail <text>`; `/uatprobe late` sends `uatprobe sync line` to its sender
     inside the command body and `uatprobe late line` to the same sender 40 ticks later; `/uatprobe late-secret`
     does the same with the later line `/uatsecret late-hunter2`; `/uatprobe console-only` replies
     `uatprobe console ok` when its sender is a `ConsoleCommandSender` and `uatprobe: run this command in the
     server console` to any other sender.
- **Catalogue rows without an environment marker (6.3.1).** A `/upm` row without an `Env:` marker that reads the
  catalogue uses the catalogue at the handed-over build's `api-url`, through the protocol v2 routes `/v1/catalogue/…`
  only (`ultitools.upm.update-check.v2`). For the 6.3.1 batch that is the staging build (handover item 1) and the
  staging catalogue (`https://api-staging.ultikits.com/v1/catalogue/`). Catalogue requests carry no credential, so
  these rows need no linked server; before the window the backend session confirms that the catalogue holds the
  modules and versions each row's Preconditions name, and a row whose catalogue state cannot be arranged is
  `blocked`, not run. The API level these rows quote is this build's: 631, accepting modules that declare
  `api-version` 630 to 631 (`ModuleApiLevels.java:41,59-75`).
- **Expected** must name an observable truth — an exact chat line, a log line, a database row,
  an inventory slot — and never the words "it works".
- **Covers** back-references a Phase 9 GUI-excluded class name; left blank when no such class
  applies.
- A row whose Preconditions name a prior row must appear after that row in file order — asserted
  mechanically: for every row, every checklist ID cited in its Preconditions cell must have a
  strictly smaller line number in this file than the row citing it (sweep class 8, D-27a).
- **Config-per-file rule (D-06):** one checklist row per `@ConfigEntity`-annotated class or per
  shipped yml file, never one row per key. This is the one deliberate exception to the "ID cites
  its `FEATURES.md` ID verbatim" rule above: a config-per-file row (ID suffixed `-yml`, e.g.
  `ultitools.config.config-yml`) aggregates every per-key `ultitools.config.<file-stem>.*` row for
  that file rather than citing a single one of them — there is no single per-key ID to cite when
  the row's job is exercising the whole file at once. A build-time (Maven-filtered) property —
  one resolved by Maven resource filtering at package time, such as `env.yml`'s `api-url` — is
  exercised by the build that produced the handed-over jar, not by editing the shipped file after
  the fact; such a row must state which build property it proves, and its Preconditions must
  state that the file it reads comes from a fresh, unmodified extraction of that build's jar at
  the property's shipped default, not a hand-edited copy.
- This repository's shipped default is `language: "zh"` in `config.yml`. Every row below whose
  Expected quotes a literal in-game or console line therefore carries the precondition
  `language: en` set in `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml`, so the observed line matches this
  document's English-only text exactly, character for character.

## /ul — framework administration

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.ul.help | `language: en` in config.yml | Run `/ul help` (console or op player) | Chat/console output is exactly `=== UltiTools Commands ===` followed by `/ul reload Reload all modules`, `/ul reload <name> Reload a specific module`, `/ul list Check loaded module list`, `================` | server | |
| ultitools.ul.list | At least one KNOWN module confirmed successfully loaded (its name appears in the server's own startup log as enabled, not merely a JAR present in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` — an invalid module, or one excluded by `PluginDependencyResolver` for a missing or circular hard dependency, leaves `PluginManager#getPluginList()` without an entry despite the JAR being present) | Run `/ul list` | One line per loaded module reading `<module name> <version>`, matching `UltiToolsPlugin#getPluginName` and `#getVersion` for every module actually present, INCLUDING a line for the known successfully-loaded module named in the precondition — a zero-line or module-missing result is a fail, not a vacuous pass | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload | Same precondition as ultitools.ul.list (appears after it), PLUS the UltiChat module specifically loaded and successfully enabled (the generic "at least one KNOWN module" precondition it inherits is not enough here — this row's second fixture needs `config/chat.yml` and its `chat.format`-driven behaviour to exist at all, which only UltiChat's own JAR provides); two concrete, named fixtures, not merely "every loaded module" in the abstract — a partial reload that updates only one module cannot be distinguished from a full reload without at least two independent fixtures: (1) the framework's own `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` `language` key, observable via `/ul help` showing the localized-vs-English block — reloaded through `UltiTools#reloadPluginsAndReport()` calling `reloadConfig()` then `initLanguage()`, NOT through `reloadSelf()`, since the framework is not itself a plugin module in `PluginManager`'s list; (2) UltiChat's `config/chat.yml` `chat.format` key, observable via a plain chat message (the same technique `ultichat.uchat.reload` uses for its own `/uchat reload` case) — reloaded through `PluginManager#reloadAllAndReport()` calling UltiChat's own `UltiToolsPlugin#reloadWithReport()`, a genuinely distinct code path from the framework's own reload above; this row is capped at proving these two named, differently-implemented fixtures both pick up their new values in one `/ul reload` pass, not literally all sixteen possible modules, and makes no claim beyond them; the server MUST be running with `language: zh` (NOT `en`) immediately before this row's own edit, so flipping it to `en` is a genuine state change rather than a no-op that would let `/ul help` show the English block even if `reloadConfig()`/`initLanguage()` never ran | Confirm `/ul help` currently shows the localized `lang/zh.json` block (proving the pre-edit state is genuinely non-English); edit both named fixtures directly on disk (`config.yml`'s `language` to `en`, `chat.yml`'s `chat.format` to a distinguishable value); run `/ul reload` once; then run `/ul help` and send a chat message to surface each fixture's new value | Before the edit, `/ul help` shows the Chinese block, not the English one; the framework's own `reloadConfig()`/`initLanguage()` pass and UltiChat's own `reloadSelf()` both run with no exception in the console; a module list taken with `/ul list` immediately after is unchanged; `/ul help` NOW shows the new English block (a genuine change from the confirmed pre-edit Chinese state, not a vacuous match) AND the sent chat message is formatted with the new `chat.format` value — either fixture failing to pick up its new value is rejected, since it cannot distinguish "every module reloaded" from "only one did"; the reply to whoever ran `/ul reload` is exactly one line, `All <n> modules reloaded.`, where `<n>` is the number of modules the `/ul list` above showed (English, because the framework's language is re-read before the modules reload) | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-module | `language: en` in config.yml; a loaded module named `<name>` (positive case: an existing module with the same kind of on-disk-value/command pairing as `ultitools.ul.reload`; negative case below: a name that does not exist) | Edit the named module's on-disk value, run `/ul reload <name>`, then run the module's command surfacing that value. Negative case: run `/ul reload does-not-exist` | Positive: chat/console line reads `Module <name> has been reloaded` (green), AND the module's command output reflects the new on-disk value. Negative: chat/console line reads `does-not-exist is not a loaded UltiTools module. Use /ul list to see loaded modules` (red) | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-log-line | `language: en` in config.yml (the Expected column asserts the literal English catalogue value; the shipped default resolves through lang/zh.json instead); same module precondition as ultitools.ul.list (appears after it); at least two modules currently loaded, with their names and count recorded from a fresh `/ul list` run immediately before this row's own steps | Run `/ul reload` from the server console and capture the full console output produced during that single command | The console output contains exactly one line per module recorded from the preceding `/ul list`, each reading, verbatim, `Module '<module name>' reloaded.` (the `lang/en.json` value of the framework's `RELOAD_LOG_MESSAGE_KEY`) — every recorded module's name appears in exactly one such line, no module is missing, and no module's name appears twice; the module's own log output (if any) is a separate matter this row makes no claim about | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-language-pending | The server running with `language: en` (started, or fully reloaded with `/ul reload`, after that value was set); UltiCleaner loaded and enabled (its name in the startup log); an op player online; record the loaded module count `<n>` from a fresh `/ul list` | Edit `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` on disk, setting `language` to `zh`, and do not run a full reload. As the op player run `/ul reload UltiCleaner`, then `/ul help`. Then run `/ul reload` and `/ul help` again. Cleanup: set `language` back to `en` on disk and run `/ul reload` | After `/ul reload UltiCleaner` the player's reply is exactly `Module UltiCleaner reloaded partially; not reloaded: the language setting in config.yml changed from en to zh; a full /ul reload applies it to every module`, the console holds a WARNING reading `Module 'UltiCleaner' reloaded partially; not reloaded: the language setting in config.yml changed from en to zh; a full /ul reload applies it to every module`, no `Module UltiCleaner has been reloaded` reply appears, and `/ul help` still shows the English block. After the full `/ul reload` the reply is `已重载全部 <n> 个模块。` (the framework now speaks `zh`) and `/ul help` shows the Chinese block. A plain success reply after the per-module reload, or the module switching language on its own, is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload-module.neg-reload-failure | `language: en` in config.yml; UltiCleaner loaded and enabled (its name in the startup log); `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/UltiCleaner/config/cleaner.yml` holds `item.interval: 300` (shipped default; its declared range is 10 to 3600); an op player online | Edit `cleaner.yml` on disk, setting `item.interval` to `5`. As the op player run `/ul reload UltiCleaner`. Cleanup: set `item.interval` back to `300` on disk and run `/ul reload UltiCleaner` again | The reply is one red line starting with `Module UltiCleaner failed to reload:`, containing `config/cleaner.yml`, and ending `See the console for details.`; no `Module UltiCleaner has been reloaded` line follows it. The console holds a SEVERE line starting with `Module 'UltiCleaner' failed to reload:` with a stack trace, and no `Module 'UltiCleaner' reloaded.` line for this reload. After the cleanup reload the reply is `Module UltiCleaner has been reloaded`. A success reply, or no reply at all, for the out-of-range value is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.ul.reload.neg-module-failure | Every precondition of ultitools.ul.reload-module.neg-reload-failure (appears earlier in this file), and at least two other modules loaded, at least one of them listed after UltiCleaner by a fresh `/ul list` (list order is load order, the order `/ul reload` visits); record the loaded module count `<n>` from that `/ul list` | Edit `cleaner.yml` on disk, setting `item.interval` to `5`. Run `/ul reload` once and capture the reply and the console output of that command. Cleanup: set `item.interval` back to `300` on disk and run `/ul reload` | The reply is exactly `Failed to reload 1 of <n> modules: UltiCleaner. See the console for each failure.`; the console holds one SEVERE line starting with `Module 'UltiCleaner' failed to reload:`, and a `Module '<name>' reloaded.` line for every other module `/ul list` showed — including every module listed after UltiCleaner — and no `All <n> modules reloaded.` line. After the cleanup reload the reply is `All <n> modules reloaded.`. A module after UltiCleaner with no reload line, or an all-reloaded reply for the failing reload, is `fail` | server | |

## /upm — plugin management

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.upm.check | `language: en` in config.yml; the startup update scan (triggered automatically at boot; watch the console until a terminal update-available/all-up-to-date line appears before running this row, rather than reading output at a fixed tick count) has already completed — `checkUpdates` only reads `UpdateManager`'s already-populated collections, it never itself queries or awaits the endpoint, so running this before that scan finishes reads the still-empty initial state; at least one module update available — this is a deliberately-installed state, not the shipped default: install an older version of a module (not the framework — `PluginInstallUtils#installPlugin` only downloads into the module folder `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, never the framework's own plugin JAR, so this recipe cannot produce a framework downgrade) with `/upm install <plugin> <version>`, choosing a `<plugin>` that is NOT already installed (no JAR for it in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; if one is there, remove it with the server stopped before this install) — `installPlugin` only adds the requested version's JAR and never moves or deletes an existing one, so an already-installed module would leave two JARs and make the reported `<current>` version unreliable — using the local update-family fixture described in the fixture note `18-FIXTURE-update-family.md` — the public catalogue holds only one version of most modules, so it cannot supply the older version (#452); the older-version install alone does NOT reach this row's positive state — `UpdateManager#checkModuleUpdates` populates `moduleUpdates` ONLY during the one-time boot scan (`checkUpdatesSync`, called once from `UltiTools#onEnable`'s startup task); `/upm check` (`PluginInstallCommands`) never itself queries the endpoint or re-compares versions on demand, it only reads those already-populated fields via `getFrameworkUpdate()`/`getModuleUpdates()`. So after installing the older fixture, RESTART THE SERVER (a fresh boot scan) and again watch the console until a terminal update-available/all-up-to-date line appears before running this row. This row's precondition is established entirely by its own fixture install and restart — it does not depend on any other row's state or ordering. Within a batch, dispatch this row AFTER that older-version install AND its required restart | Run `/upm check` | Console/chat shows `Available updates:` followed by one `<name> <current> → <latest>` line per available update — `checkUpdates` formats the transition with a Unicode arrow `→`, not the ASCII sequence `->` | server | |
| ultitools.upm.check.neg-none | `language: en` in config.yml; the startup update scan (triggered automatically at boot; watch the console until a terminal line appears, as above) has already completed and ended with `[UltiTools-API] All plugins are up to date!` (as of 6.3.0 printed only when every lookup was answered; a failed lookup ends the scan with `Update check incomplete: …` instead, and `/upm check` then repeats that sentence rather than the line below); no update available for any module or the framework — this IS the batch's default install state: within a batch, dispatch this row BEFORE the positive sibling row immediately above installs its own older-version fixture, since that install makes this row's own precondition false until the module or framework is updated back to latest | Run `/upm check` | Chat/console line reads `No updates available.` (green) | protocol | |
| ultitools.upm.help | `language: en` in config.yml | Run `/upm help` | Chat/console shows `========\|Plugin Install Help\|========` followed by eight usage lines, all green, starting with `/upm list [page] - View available plugin list` and ending with `/upm update all - Update all plugins`. This path has no `@CmdMapping` site — `BaseCommandExecutor#onCommand` matches the literal `help` argument and dispatches straight to `handleHelp` | server | |
| ultitools.upm.help.neg-bare | `language: en` in config.yml | Run bare `/upm` with no arguments | A preceding red `Unknown command, please enter /upm help for help` line, THEN the same help block as `ultitools.upm.help` — `matchMethod` returns null for zero args, so `onCommand` sends the unknown-command message before falling through to the same `handleHelp` call; the two invocations are not identical | server | |
| ultitools.upm.install | `language: en` in config.yml; the server runs this UltiTools-API 6.3.1 build (API level 631, so it installs modules declaring `api-version` 630 to 631); `<plugin>` exists in the catalogue (Conventions, catalogue rows) with a version whose JAR declares `api-version: 630` or `631` and is not yet installed; you know its identify-string lower-cased as `<id>` | Run `/upm install <plugin>` | Chat/console line reads `Installed! Please restart the server! Please be sure to delete the old version module!` (green); the module JAR `<id>-<version>.jar` (`<version>` the catalogue's latest version for API level 631) now exists under `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, and no file there ends in `.part` | server | |

| ultitools.upm.list | `language: en` in config.yml; locate the catalogue page that lists a currently-loaded module — as a player, page through `/upm list <page>` starting from page 1 until an entry's install-state marker reads `installed`; that page, not necessarily page 1 (the live catalogue's own ordering is outside this project's control), is what the Steps below check for the marker-varies requirement — installing only downloads the JAR, `listPlugins` determines installed/not-installed from `PluginManager#getPluginList()` (the currently-loaded modules), so a newly-downloaded-but-not-yet-loaded module still reads `not installed`, and a fresh install must be followed by a server RESTART, confirmed via `/ul list` (or the startup log), before it reads `installed` — that restart-to-`installed` clause needs a catalogue serving a release of the module built for this UltiTools-API (as of 6.3.0 `/upm install` itself refuses a release built for an earlier API level after downloading it and writes nothing, so such an entry never reads `installed`); if the page found this way shows no entry marked `not installed` (all ten entries on it already loaded), uninstall one of the other entries on that same page by its resolved runtime name — use that entry's own uninstall click action from the player output, or the name shown in `/ul list`, NOT the catalogue `<id>` used for `/upm install`: `PluginInstallUtils#uninstallPlugin` matches only `getPluginName()`/the JAR's `plugin.yml` name, never the catalogue identifier — then RESTART THE SERVER, and confirm via `/ul list` that it no longer appears, so that page shows both states before running the Steps below | Run `/upm list` as a player, then separately as console; then, as a player, run `/upm list <page>` for the page located in the Preconditions above | The bare `/upm list` call (page 1) ends with `======== Page 1 ========` in both cases, listing AT LEAST ONE named entry — this is itself the proof the underlying catalogue request `/v1/catalogue/modules?page=1&pageSize=10` (`CatalogueClient.java:171-173`) succeeded: `PluginInstallUtils#getPluginList` returns an empty list on any non-2xx or malformed response, and the for-loop below `listPlugins`'s header renders nothing for an empty list, so an empty page (header/footer only, zero entries) can never satisfy "AT LEAST ONE named entry" — no separate network capture is needed to distinguish "the catalogue loaded" from "the request failed and the loop ran zero times". As a player, each entry carries an install-state marker of literal text `installed` or `not installed` (each preceded by a single space in the actual output); console output carries no such marker at all, on page 1 or any other page — each entry lists only the name, an `Install Command: /upm install <id>` line (ASCII colon followed by one space, the `lang/en.json` value verbatim), and the description. On the page located in the Preconditions above — the page listing the currently-loaded module, not necessarily page 1 — the player-branch output shows at least one entry reading `installed` and at least one reading `not installed`, proving the marker actually varies with real state rather than always printing the same value. Fixed #439: `/upm list` prints the catalogue's `name` field, which for the loaded `UltiTools-Economy`/`UltiTools-Menu` modules (their runtime `plugin.yml` `name:`) is `UltiEconomy` and `UltiMenu` — neither equals the runtime name; if that page includes the `UltiEconomy` or `UltiMenu` entry, that entry's marker reads `installed` — not `not installed` — while a catalogue entry for a module that is genuinely not loaded still reads `not installed`. On the live catalogue that match comes from the identify string both sides carry (`ultieconomy`, `ultimenu`); #439's prefix-stripping path (a catalogue name equal to the runtime name minus `UltiTools-`, e.g. `Economy`) runs only when one side has no identify string, which no live catalogue entry and no current module meets, so it is unit-covered only — `PluginInstallCommandsTest#matchesTheTwoNamedModulesByPrefixNormalisation` — and is not asserted at this row's `server` layer. (The rejection of a catalogue name that merely resembles a loaded module's without being it, e.g. one ending in `Pro`, is likewise unit-covered only — `PluginInstallCommandsTest#resemblingButDifferentModuleIsRejected` — since no such entry is confirmed to exist on the live catalogue.) | server | |
| ultitools.upm.list-page | `language: en` in config.yml; the catalogue has more than one page; network-capture-confirm that the page-2 catalogue request `/v1/catalogue/modules?page=2&pageSize=10` returns an HTTP 2xx with at least one entry, for the same reason as `ultitools.upm.list` above — `PluginInstallUtils#getPluginList` returns an empty list on a non-2xx or malformed response, and `listPlugins` still prints the requested page's footer regardless, so an empty failed page 2 would satisfy this row's footer/differs-from-page-1 check without retrieving any page-2 data | Run `/upm list 2`, with the confirmed-successful page-2 response above | The footer line reads `======== Page 2 ========`, listing AT LEAST ONE named entry from the confirmed response, and the listed entries differ from page 1's | server | |

| ultitools.upm.list.click-buttons | Every precondition of ultitools.upm.list (appears earlier in this file): `language: en` in config.yml, and the page located there shows at least one entry reading ` installed` and at least one reading ` not installed`; an OP player in a real client whose received chat packets are captured (raw system-chat component, not the rendered text) | As that player run `/upm list <page>` for that page and keep the received packets; then click the `     \| Install \|     ` button under one ` not installed` entry and watch the server console. From Minecraft 1.21.6 the client may show a confirmation screen before it runs a clicked command; if it does, confirm it — that screen is vanilla client behaviour and is neither a pass nor a fail on its own | The whole reply arrives as ONE system-chat message, not one message per line (a reply split into one message per line is the legacy-text fallback that drops click events, and is a fail on its own). Inside it, the `     \| Uninstall \|     ` component under every ` installed` entry carries a `run_command` click event whose command is exactly `/upm uninstall <runtime name>` — the loaded module's `plugin.yml` `name:` as `/ul list` prints it (e.g. `UltiTools-Economy` for the catalogue entry `UltiEconomy`) — and hover text `Click to uninstall module`; the `     \| Install \|     ` component under every ` not installed` entry carries a `run_command` click event `/upm install <identify-string>` and hover text `Click to install module`. A button carrying text and colour only, with no click event, is a fail, whatever the rest of the row shows. Clicking the Install button makes the console print `<player> issued server command: /upm install <identify-string>`, and the install reply appears in the clicking player's chat (a `/upm` reply goes to the command's sender, so it is not printed on the console); no such console line after the click is a fail. Cleanup: before the next restart, delete the downloaded file from `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` — the install reply names no file; the download is `<identify-string>-<version>.jar` (the lower-cased identify string and the catalogue version), the one file present there after the click that was absent before it. Fixed in 6.3.0: on Paper 1.21.11 the buttons used to arrive with no click event, so clicking them did nothing | java-client | |

| ultitools.cloud.command.neg-unknown | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`; `ultipanel.commands.blocklist` at its shipped default; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; `messages.unknown-command` in `spigot.yml` at its shipped value | 1. ## Boot sequence and listeners

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.boot.deferred-removal | `language: en` in config.yml; ultitools.upm.uninstall.deferred (appears earlier in this file) has just passed; the server has then been stopped and `chmod u+w plugins/UltiTools/plugins` run | Start the server and read the start-up log; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` and `.ultikits/upm-transactions/`; run `/ul list` | The log shows, before the module loading lines, one INFO line reading `Deleted the module JAR files an uninstall could not delete: <plugins folder path>/plugins/<the module's JAR file name>.`; the JAR is gone, `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` holds no record, and `/ul list` does not list the module | server | |
| ultitools.boot.deferred-removal.unreadable | `language: en` in config.yml; a POSIX host; the server process does not run as root (`ps -o user= -p <server pid>` does not print `root`; file permissions are not enforced for root); ultitools.upm.uninstall.deferred (appears earlier in this file) has passed again from a fresh fixture state, so `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` holds one `remove-*.json` and the module's JAR `<jar>` is still in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; a copy of that JAR kept outside the server folder; the server has then been stopped and `chmod u+w plugins/UltiTools/plugins` run | 1. Run `chmod a-r plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>` and confirm, as the server's user, that `cat plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar> > /dev/null` fails with `Permission denied` (if it succeeds, restore the permission and record `blocked`); run `sha256sum .ultikits/upm-transactions/remove-*.json` and note the value as `<record-hash>`. 2. Start the server and read the start-up log; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` and `.ultikits/upm-transactions/`; run `sha256sum .ultikits/upm-transactions/remove-*.json` again. 3. Stop the server, run `chmod u+r plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>`, start the server and read the start-up log; list both folders again. Cleanup: stop the server, copy the kept copy of the module's JAR back into `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, start the server | Step 2: the log shows one SEVERE line reading `The module JAR <plugins folder path>/plugins/<jar> recorded by an uninstall could not be read to confirm it is still the recorded file (AccessDeniedException: plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>); it was not deleted, loads again at this start, and the next start tries again.` (the path inside the parentheses is the one the Java exception reports, in the form the Expected of `ultitools.upm.uninstall.deferred` describes), and no line reading `Deleted the module JAR files an uninstall could not delete: …` and none containing `has been replaced since`; `<jar>` is still in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` still holds the one `remove-*.json`, and its `sha256sum` equals `<record-hash>`. Any other line the start prints about that unreadable file failing to load as a module is not judged by this row. Step 3: the log shows one INFO line reading `Deleted the module JAR files an uninstall could not delete: <plugins folder path>/plugins/<jar>.`; `<jar>` is gone and `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` holds no record. A deletion or a missing or changed record after step 2, a `has been replaced since` WARNING, or no deletion after step 3 is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.boot.duplicate-module-jars | `language: en` in config.yml; a module is installed whose JAR file name `<jar>` sorts before `zz-duplicate-copy.jar` in plain code-point order (for example, it starts with an ASCII letter or digit); you know its `plugin.yml` `main:` value `<main>` (read it with `unzip -p plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar> plugin.yml`) | Stop the server; copy `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<jar>` to `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/zz-duplicate-copy.jar`; start the server and read the console output during startup; run `/ul list`. Cleanup: stop the server, delete `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/zz-duplicate-copy.jar`, start the server | The console shows exactly one WARNING line reading `[UltiTools-API] These JAR files all declare the same module main class <main>: <jar>, zz-duplicate-copy.jar. The module's classes load only from <jar>; the other copies are not loaded. Keep only one of them.`, and no SEVERE line naming `zz-duplicate-copy.jar`; `/ul list` lists the module once. A second line naming both files, a SEVERE line for the copy, or the module listed twice is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.boot.duplicate-module-jars.neg-none | `language: en` in config.yml; ultitools.boot.duplicate-module-jars (appears earlier in this file) has just passed and its cleanup start has run | Read the console output of that cleanup start | No line containing `declare the same module main class` appears | server | |
| ultitools.boot.load-summary | `language: en` in config.yml; the server is stopped; `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` holds `<n>` module JARs (`<n>` at least 1) built for this UltiTools-API that all load — a start before this row printed `[UltiTools-API] Succeeded loaded <n>, Failed 0.` and `/ul list` listed `<n>` modules; beside them, copy in by hand one module JAR built for an older UltiTools-API whose main class overrides `UltiToolsPlugin.unregisterSelf()` — any module JAR declaring `api-version: 620`, for example the catalogue's `ultirecipe-1.0.0.jar` (`/upm install` refuses such a module as of 6.3.0, so it is copied in, not installed) — and note its file name `<old-jar>` | Start the server and read the console output during startup; run `/ul list`. | ultitools.boot.module-update-apply.neg-apply-failed | `language: en` in config.yml; a POSIX host; the server process does not run as root (`ps -o user= -p <server pid>` does not print `root`; file permissions are not enforced for root); ultitools.upm.update (appears earlier in this file) has passed again for a module, from a fresh fixture state; the server is stopped and `chmod a-w plugins/UltiTools/plugins` has been run | Start the server and read the start-up log; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; run `/ul list`; then stop the server, run `chmod u+w plugins/UltiTools/plugins`, start it, and run `/upm update <module-name>` | The start-up log shows one SEVERE line beginning `The update of module <module-name> (<current> -> <latest>) could not be applied: <path of the previous JAR>: AccessDeniedException` and ending `The modules folder is unchanged and <current> loads at this start; the next /upm update of it reports this again.`; `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` still holds the previous JAR and no `-<latest>.jar`; `/ul list` shows the module at `<current>`. Cleanup: stop the server, then run `rm plugins/UltiTools/plugins && mv /dev/shm/uat-modules plugins/UltiTools/plugins` | The start-up log shows one SEVERE line reading exactly `The update of module <module-name> (<current> -> <latest>) could not be applied: the modules folder <server root>/plugins/UltiTools/plugins and the update folder <server root>/.ultikits/upm-transactions are on different file systems, so the files cannot be swapped by an atomic move. The modules folder is unchanged and <current> loads at this start; put both folders on one file system, then run /upm update again.`; `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` still holds the previous JAR and no `-<latest>.jar`; the staged `-<latest>.jar` is still under `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` (no copy of it was made); `/ul list` shows the module at `<current>`. A green line, a `-<latest>.jar` in the modules folder, or a SEVERE line that names only one of the two folders is `fail` | server | |
| ultitools.boot.module-update-apply.neg-unconfirmed | `language: en` in config.yml; a local catalogue serves, as `<latest>` of a module, a build of that module whose `registerSelf()` waits 60 seconds before returning (a fixture built for this row, so the window below is wide enough to act in); `/upm update <module-name>` has replied `Update of <module-name> staged (…)`; you can kill the server's Java process from a shell | Start the server; after the console prints `[UltiTools-API] Found` and before it prints `[UltiTools-API] Plugin Loading completed.` (the fixture holds it there for 60 seconds), kill the Java process with `kill -9 <pid>`; list `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; then start the server again normally and run `/ul list` | After the kill, `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` holds the `-<latest>.jar` (the files were swapped before loading). Cleanup: stop the server, delete `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<id>-<latest>.jar`, delete the `update-*.json` record and the `update-*` folder of the same name under `.ultikits/upm-transactions/`, start the server | The first start's log shows one SEVERE line beginning `The update of module <module-name> is on hold for the operator: <server root>/plugins/UltiTools/plugins/<id>-<latest>.jar (expected no file, found SHA-256 <found>).` and ending `then delete the update record <server root>/.ultikits/upm-transactions/update-<hash>.json and the folder of the same name to give up this update.`; `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` still holds `<jar>` and the copy, unchanged; `.ultikits/upm-transactions/` still holds the record (its `state` is `NEEDS_OPERATOR`) and the staged JAR. `/upm update <module-name>` prints, in red, a line beginning `The update of <module-name> was not staged: an update of this module is on hold for the operator (` and ending `Nothing was changed.`; `/upm uninstall <module-name>` prints, in red, a line beginning `Refusing to uninstall <module-name>: an update of it is on hold for the operator (` and ending `Nothing was unloaded or deleted`; `/ul list` still lists the module at `<current>`. The second start's log shows the same SEVERE line again; the module still loads from the old JAR (its normal enabled and loaded lines appear); the existing duplicate-copy WARNING `These JAR files all declare the same module main class … Keep only one of them.` naming both the old JAR and `<id>-<latest>.jar` is expected, because the held update leaves the copy in the modules folder; no transaction change (the record, its `state`, the staged JAR and the copy are unchanged, as above). | ultitools.boot.paper-floor | Two fresh throwaway servers, each in its own empty directory and never started before: Paper 1.19.2 build 307 run on Java 17, and Paper 1.21.11 run on Java 21. No `language: en` step, deliberately: every line quoted below is a fixed English string in the source, not a translated message, and the configuration files compared must be exactly the ones a first start writes | On each server: start it once and wait for the console line containing `Done (`; run `stop` and wait for the process to exit. Read both start-up logs | On both servers the framework enables (no line containing `Error occurred while enabling UltiTools`) and the console prints `[UltiTools-API] Succeeded loaded 3, Failed 0.`. The 1.19.2 log has no line containing `was not written`. The two sorted path lists are identical and each has exactly 13 entries, and all 13 `cmp` runs exit 0. On 1.19.2 exactly these six lines are expected and are not failures; each quoted text is how the message begins right after the time-and-level prefix (`[<time> WARN]: ` or `[<time> ERROR]: ` on the console, `[<time>] [<thread>/WARN]: ` or `[<time>] [<thread>/ERROR]: ` in `logs/latest.log`): (1) one WARNING beginning `[UltiTools] [UltiPanel] Paper's log forwarder (org.bukkit.craftbukkit.util.ForwardLogHandler) was not found on this server, so the panel will not mirror the server console`; (2) two WARNINGs beginning `[com.ultikits.ultitools.context.FinalContractValidator] cannot check @Final method contract for com.ultikits.plugins.login.listener.LoginProtection`, one ending `LoginProtectionListener: org/bukkit/event/entity/EntityMountEvent is not on the classpath` and one ending `LoginProtectionPaperListener: io/papermc/paper/event/player/PlayerPickItemEvent is not on the classpath`; (3) two WARNINGs beginning `[com.ultikits.ultitools.context.SimpleContainer] Skipping bean 'loginProtection`, one for `'loginProtectionPaperListener'` and one for `'loginProtectionListener'`, each ending `-- its class references a symbol absent from the classpath`; (4) one ERROR beginning `[UltiTools] Module 'UltiLogin' skipped 2 class(es) that failed to load during startup` and naming `com.ultikits.plugins.login.listener.LoginProtectionPaperListener` and `com.ultikits.plugins.login.listener.LoginProtectionListener`. Each of the four WARNINGs in (2) and (3) is followed by a multi-line stack trace whose first line is `java.lang.NoClassDefFoundError: ` and the same missing class (`org/bukkit/event/entity/EntityMountEvent` for the `LoginProtectionListener` lines, `io/papermc/paper/event/player/PlayerPickItemEvent` for the `LoginProtectionPaperListener` lines); those trace lines are part of the expected output. Any other WARNING or ERROR naming UltiTools or one of the three modules that appears on 1.19.2 and not on 1.21.11 is a fail, and so is an extra copy of any line above or a `NoClassDefFoundError` trace naming any other class. This row proves the framework's floor only; it does not prove that every module GUI works on 1.19.2 (UltiKits, UltiLogin, UltiMail, UltiRemoteBag and UltiTrade need Minecraft 1.21+) | server | |
| ultitools.boot.plugin-load-order | At least two modules installed with a declared hard `@PluginDependency` between them; the server is NOT launched with `-Dultitools.useLegacyPluginLoading=true` (that flag bypasses dependency resolution entirely, producing a different console signature than this row's Expected) | Restart the server and read the console output during startup | Console shows `[UltiTools-API] Plugin load order resolved successfully.`, and the dependent module's own `onEnable` log line appears after its dependency's | server | |

A module that only lists the refused module under `softDepends`, `loadBefore` or `plugin.yml` `loadAfter` is not excluded (see `ultitools.boot.plugin-load-order.neg-missing-optional-dependents`) | server | |

Kahn's algorithm never assigns a node downstream of the cycle an in-degree of 0, so a module that depends on a cycle member is excluded too, not just the two modules forming the cycle itself | server | |

## XSeries lookups

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.xseries.item-lookup | A Paper 1.21.11 server (UltiRemoteBag needs Minecraft 1.21+) with the framework JAR handed over for this dispatch in `plugins/` and an UltiRemoteBag JAR built from its `master` branch in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`; a player in a real client who holds `ultibag.use` and whose received packets are captured and decoded (the container-contents packet, not the rendered screen) | As that player run `/bag 1` and keep the container-contents packet sent for the window that opens. Read the server console from the command until the window is open | The window is the six-row (54-slot) content page of page 1. Zero-based window slots 46, 47, 51 and 52 (columns 1, 2, 6 and 7 of the bottom row) each hold exactly one item with id `minecraft:gray_stained_glass_pane`. An item with id `minecraft:glass_pane` in any of those four slots is a fail: that is the fallback `XVersionUtils#getColoredPlaneGlass` uses when the XSeries lookup finds nothing. No console line names `com.cryptomorin.xseries` or shows a `NoClassDefFoundError` or `ExceptionInInitializerError` | java-client | |
| ultitools.xseries.sound-lookup | A Paper 1.21.11 server with the framework JAR handed over for this dispatch in `plugins/` and an UltiSocial JAR built from its `master` branch as the only module in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`, so the framework's own `TeleportService` is the one UltiSocial's `FriendCommand` receives (with none, `/friend tp` teleports without any sound, and another module's teleport service would not be this feature); UltiSocial's `tp_to_friend.enabled` at its default `true`; two players A and B in real clients, both holding `ultisocial.use`, made friends with `/friend add B` run by A and `/friend accept A` run by B, standing at least 10 blocks apart; A has not used `/friend tp` within UltiSocial's `tp_to_friend.cooldown`; A's received packets are captured and decoded | As A run `/friend tp B` and keep the packets A receives in the following second. Read the server console for the same second | A is moved to B's location: A's position, read on the server right after the command, is within 1 block of B's. Among the packets A receives right after the teleport is a sound packet whose sound decodes to `minecraft:entity.enderman.teleport` with volume 1.0; the pitch is not asserted, because the client clamps the requested 0. No such sound packet after the teleport is a fail. No console line names `com.cryptomorin.xseries` or shows an exception from the teleport | java-client | |
| ultitools.xseries.sound-lookup.paper-floor | ultitools.boot.paper-floor (appears earlier in this file) has passed. A fresh throwaway Paper 1.19.2 build 307 server run on Java 17, with the framework JAR handed over for this dispatch in `plugins/` and an UltiSocial JAR built from its `master` branch as the only module in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` (UltiSocial is not one of the five modules that need Minecraft 1.21+, and with no other module the framework's own `TeleportService` is the one UltiSocial's `FriendCommand` receives); the server console after start shows `[UltiTools-API] Succeeded loaded 1, Failed 0.`; UltiSocial's `tp_to_friend.enabled` at its default `true`; two players A and B in real Minecraft 1.19.2 clients, both holding `ultisocial.use`, made friends with `/friend add B` run by A and `/friend accept A` run by B, standing at least 10 blocks apart; A has not used `/friend tp` within UltiSocial's `tp_to_friend.cooldown`; A's received packets are captured and decoded | As A run `/friend tp B` and keep the packets A receives in the following second. Read the server console for the same second | A is moved to B's location: A's position, read on the server right after the command, is within 1 block of B's. Among the packets A receives right after the teleport is a sound packet whose sound decodes to `minecraft:entity.enderman.teleport` with volume 1.0; the pitch is not asserted, because the client clamps the requested 0. No such sound packet after the teleport is a fail: on this server it would mean the shipped XSeries could not resolve the sound by name against the 1.19.2 API, and `XVersionUtils#getSound` returned no sound. No console line names `com.cryptomorin.xseries` or shows a `NoClassDefFoundError`, `NoSuchMethodError`, `NoSuchFieldError` or `ExceptionInInitializerError`, or any other exception from the teleport | java-client | |

## Economy

All five rows share one real, verifiable trigger fixture rather than an artificial call site:
UltiEssentials' `DeathPunishListener`, whose `deathPunishMoneyEnabled` config key gates a call to
`EconomyUtils.isAvailable()` on every player death — the same check every other economy-consuming
module in this codebase performs before touching a balance (measured: `UltiEssentials`,
`UltiKits`, `UltiRemoteBag` all gate the same way), so a warning that fires here is proven against
a real module's real call pattern, not a synthetic one.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.economy.report-startup-state.neg-vault-absent | `language: en` in config.yml; no plugin named `Vault` installed on the server; UltiEssentials installed and enabled with `deathPunishMoneyEnabled: true` in its own config; UltiEconomy removed from `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/` for this row and put back afterwards (it declares `depend: [ Vault ]`, so without Vault it is refused by design, see `ultitools.boot.missing-required-plugin`) | Restart the server and read the console startup log; then let any player die once | Console startup log shows exactly `[UltiTools-API] Vault not installed - economy service unavailable.`; every installed module — including UltiEssentials — still appears in `/ul list` and finishes enabling, proving the framework's own bootstrap does not crash on a server with no Vault plugin (issue #451, previously a total load failure here); after the death, console shows exactly one WARNING line reading `[UltiTools-API] Module 'UltiEssentials' requested the economy service, but Vault is not installed on this server. This is the server's environment, not a defect in UltiTools or in 'UltiEssentials'. Install Vault and an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) that registers a Vault economy provider to enable economy features.` | server | |
| ultitools.economy.report-startup-state.neg-no-provider | `language: en` in config.yml; Vault plugin installed and enabled; no economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) installed, so no `Economy` provider is registered with Vault; UltiEssentials installed and enabled with `deathPunishMoneyEnabled: true` in its own config; UltiEconomy removed for this row and put back afterwards (it registers itself as a Vault economy provider, `UltiEconomy.java:122-123`, so with it installed a provider exists) | Restart the server and read the console startup log; then let any player die once | Console startup log shows exactly `[UltiTools-API] Vault detected, but no economy provider is registered - economy service unavailable.`; every installed module, including Vault itself and UltiEssentials, finishes enabling; after the death, console shows exactly one WARNING line reading `[UltiTools-API] Module 'UltiEssentials' requested the economy service, but Vault is installed, but no economy provider is registered with it. This is the server's environment, not a defect in UltiTools or in 'UltiEssentials'. Install Vault and an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) that registers a Vault economy provider to enable economy features.` | server | |
| ultitools.economy.report-startup-state | `language: en` in config.yml; Vault plugin installed and enabled; an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) installed and enabled, registering an `Economy` provider with Vault; UltiEssentials installed and enabled with `deathPunishMoneyEnabled: true` in its own config | Restart the server and read the console startup log; then let any player die once, checking the player's balance via the registered economy provider's own command (e.g. `/balance` for EssentialsX) immediately before and immediately after the death | Console startup log shows `[UltiTools-API] Hooked into Vault, economy provider: <provider name>.` naming the actual registered provider; after the death, NO `[UltiTools-API] Module '...' requested the economy service` WARNING line appears for UltiEssentials, and the player's balance after the death is strictly lower than before it — the death-punish money deduction proceeds normally instead of being silently skipped | server | |
| ultitools.economy.report-unavailable.neg-vault-absent | `language: en` in config.yml; no plugin named `Vault` installed on the server; UltiEssentials installed and enabled with `deathPunishMoneyEnabled: true` in its own config; the server has just been (re)started into this state and no economy request has been made by UltiEssentials since that restart, so `EconomyUtils`'s per-module warned-once state for UltiEssentials (`warnedModules`) is still clear — the same environment as `ultitools.economy.report-startup-state.neg-vault-absent` above, exercised as a fresh, self-contained restart rather than by relying on that row's own death having already fired the warning | Let a player die once, then let a player (the same one or a different one) die a second time in the same server session, without restarting in between | After the first death, console shows exactly one WARNING line reading `[UltiTools-API] Module 'UltiEssentials' requested the economy service, but Vault is not installed on this server. This is the server's environment, not a defect in UltiTools or in 'UltiEssentials'. Install Vault and an economy plugin (e.g. EssentialsX) that registers a Vault economy provider to enable economy features.`; after the second death, NO further WARNING line for UltiEssentials appears — `EconomyUtils.reportEconomyStateIfUnavailable` warns each calling module at most once per server session (`warnedModules` is never cleared outside of `EconomyUtils#reset()`, which only test code calls), by design, not a defect | server | |

## Scheduled tasks

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.task.player-cache-sweep | A module bean using `@PlayerCache` AND implementing `PlayerCacheManager.ExpiringPlayerCache` is registered, with at least one expired entry from a player who is no longer online — `sweepExpiredEntries` invokes `sweepExpired()` only on beans implementing that interface; a bean without it is never swept by this task | Wait for one 5-minute sweep interval | No `ConcurrentModificationException` or `Error running expiry sweep for <class>` warning appears in the console under this normal-operation pass; the bean's own `sweepExpired()` runs and the expired entry is gone from its tracked state afterward | server | |
| ultitools.task.player-cache-sweep.neg-hook-throws | Same as the row above, but the bean's `sweepExpired()` implementation is made to throw during the sweep pass (a forced-failure build/config, not normal operation) | Wait for one 5-minute sweep interval | An `Error running expiry sweep for <class>` WARNING appears in the console, naming the throwing bean's class; that bean's expired entry is NOT removed from its tracked state (the hook aborted before completing), but the sweep pass itself continues — a different bean's own `sweepExpired()` in the same pass, if registered, still runs and its own expired entry is still removed | server | |

## Module command and task runtime

The three reply rows use UltiWorlds' `/world delete world`, which never deletes anything here: a command block or an
RCON client is refused by the command body itself (only a player or the server console may delete a world), and the
console, which the panel's remote command runs as, is refused because `world` is the default world. Retired for
6.3.1: `ultitools.runtime.panel-reply` (it read the v1 `command_result` text and the v1 log stream; the protocol v2
`command.result` carries the command's captured output, which `ultitools.cloud.command.ack-result-output` and
`ultitools.cloud.command.late-output` prove).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.runtime.block-reply | `language: en` in config.yml; command blocks enabled: on Paper 1.21.11 that is the game rule `command_blocks_work` (`/gamerule command_blocks_work` reads `true`; if not, set it for this row and put it back afterwards). Paper 1.21.11 no longer reads `enable-command-block` from `server.properties`; UltiWorlds loaded; an operator in game | 1. `/setblock <x> <y> <z> minecraft:command_block{Command:"world delete world",TrackOutput:1b}`, then power it by placing a redstone block against it (`/setblock <x+1> <y> <z> minecraft:redstone_block`). 2. `/data get block <x> <y> <z> LastOutput`. 3. Control: remove the redstone block, `/data merge block <x> <y> <z> {Command:"minecraft:list"}`, place the redstone block again, and repeat step 2 | Step 2: `LastOutput` contains `Only a player or the server console can delete a world.` (the UltiWorlds body's reply; before 6.3.0 `LastOutput` was absent). Step 3: `LastOutput` holds the vanilla `list` reply, proving output tracking works on this block. Vanilla stores it as a translatable component, not as rendered text: it shows `translate: "commands.list.players"` with `with: [<n>, <m>, ...]` (the online count, the maximum, then the player names), and no English sentence; the rendered `There are <n> of a max of <m> players online` appears only in a client or the console, so its absence from `LastOutput` is not a fail | server | |

## Module lifecycle registries

The supersede and post-load attribution rows are unit-pinned: only code calling `PluginManager#register(...)` with a newer
instance of a loaded module reaches the supersede path (measured, plan 17-79).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.lifecycle.supersede-releases-registrations | not covered — unit-pinned. The only caller of `PluginManager#register` in the framework is the start-up scan, which refuses a second jar of a loaded module (#483), and no operator command registers a module at run time. Accepted limitation (maintainer decision of 2026-10-07): documented and closed on the unit test under Expected, not run on a real server | — | Held by `SupersedeRegistrationHandoverTracerTest`: the newer copy loads, answers the panel type the older copy held and owns the completer, the older copy's subscription receives nothing, and all three were already released when the newer copy's `registerSelf()` ran (`newerCopyClaimsWhatTheOlderCopyHeld`); a newer copy whose `registerSelf()` returns `false` or throws leaves the older copy loaded, answering the type with its own responder, owning the completer and receiving events, with nothing of the failed copy left registered and `M load failed` logged (`newerCopyReturningFalseGivesTheRegistrationsBack`, `newerCopyThrowingGivesTheRegistrationsBack`) | server | |
| ultitools.lifecycle.post-load-registration-attributed | not covered — unit-pinned. The release this row changes is observable only when a second copy of a module shares the name (a supersede or two listed copies), which no operator action produces, and none of the fifteen modules calls `registerResponder` or `EventBus#subscribe` (measured on #562). Accepted limitation (maintainer decision of 2026-10-07): documented and closed on the unit tests under Expected, not run on a real server | — | Held by `PostLoadRegistrationAttributionTest`: a responder and a subscription registered by name from another thread after load leave with the superseded copy (`lateRegistrationsLeaveWithTheSupersededCopy`); during a supersede the loading thread's registration is the newer copy's and another thread's the older copy's (`duringASupersedeTheScopeDecidesAndOtherwiseTheOlderCopy`); with two copies listed it leaves with the first listed (`twoListedCopiesAttributeToTheFirstListed`); one copy's unload releases it (`oneLoadedCopyReleasesItsLateRegistrationOnUnload`); a name with no loaded copy stays name-only (`aNameWithNoLoadedCopyStaysNameOnly`) | server | |
| ultitools.lifecycle.reload-keeps-registrations | Env: `stub`; `language: en` and `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true` in config.yml (the shipped default is `false`, and step 6's remote command is refused without it); the fifteen modules at the build under test loaded (a fresh `/ul list` names fifteen; record the names); UltiBackup among them; an op player `<op>` who carries no explicit negative permission node (use a test-player name that has no permission-plugin data, then `op` it: with LuckPerms an explicit `*`=false node on a player overrides operator status, and Paper then answers `/backup` with its own unknown-command line) and a second player `<p2>` online; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected | 1. Run `/ul reload` (console). 2. Run `/ul list`. 3. As `<op>`, type `/backup admin ` and press Tab. 4. As `<op>`, run `/backup list`. 5. As `<op>`, run `/ul help`. 6. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"command.execute","payload":{"command":"list"}}`; record the admin answer's `frame.id`, then read `GET /_stub/frames?type=command.result` for the frame whose `replyTo` matches that id, and the server console | Step 1: the closing summary is `All 15 modules reloaded.` and no module is named as failed. Step 2: the same fifteen names as before the reload. Step 3: the suggestions include `<p2>` (UltiBackup's `suggestOnlinePlayers` completer still answers). Step 4: either `=== Your Backups ===` or `You have no backups.` (UltiBackup's own lines), not an unknown-command or command-error line. Step 5: the `=== UltiTools Commands ===` block of `ultitools.ul.help`. Step 6: the server console logs `[UltiTools] [Remote command] > list` once, and the newest `command.result` correlated to the request is `ok` with `data.output` holding the server's own `list` reply naming `<op>` and `<p2>` (`RemoteCommandHandler.java:66-72`): the remote command still executes after the reload. As of 6.3.1 the reply is captured for the result and is not printed to the console (`CommandExecutionManager#executeCaptured`; only output printed after the dispatch returned goes to the console, see `ultitools.cloud.command.late-output`). Do not run a console `list` within the same second as step 6: its reply would look like the remote one. Control: none of the fifteen modules registers a panel responder, a programmatic EventBus subscription or a completer key (measured at plan 17-79: no `registerResponder`, `subscribe(`, `@ModuleEventHandler` or `TabCompletionManager` use under any module's `src/main`; control `registerSelf` hits 34 files), so their registrations are commands and `@CmdParam(suggest)` methods, which steps 3–5 sample | server | |

| ultitools.storage.detached-reads | not covered — unit-pinned. No shipped module changes a loaded entity without calling `update(...)` (the only code path that would show the difference), so the row cannot be exercised through any command this repository or the fifteen modules ship | — | Held by `DetachedReadParityTest` (`getAllReturnsDetachedCopies`, `getAllWithConditionsReturnsDetachedCopies`, `getByIdReturnsDetachedCopy`, `pageReturnsDetachedCopies`, `getLikeReturnsDetachedCopies`, `queryFirstReturnsDetachedCopy`, `insertedInstanceIsDetached`, `existMatchesByIdAfterALocalChange`, `updatePersistsTheChange`): each runs the same scenario on the JSON backend and on the relational backend, and a change made to a returned entity without `update(...)` leaves a fresh `getById` unchanged on both, `exist(entity)` still finds the row by its id after such a change on both, and `update(...)` persists it on both | server | |
| ultitools.storage.json-transactions | `language: en` in config.yml; `datasource.type: json` and `datasource.flushRate: 3600` in config.yml (so no background flush empties the journal during the run), and the SHA-256 of config.yml recorded after those two values are set and before step 1 (`sha256sum plugins/UltiTools/config.yml`); two probe module JARs built for this run in a scratch project outside this repository, against the UltiTools-API JAR under test, and never committed, shipped or published, both placed in `plugins/UltiTools/plugins/`: (a) a transactional probe, module name `JtxTxProbe`, whose `@Service` has two `@Transactional` methods writing the row `k` to two tables `jtx_a` and `jtx_b` (a `value` column), one that returns and one that throws a `RuntimeException` after both writes, each write logging `jtx-probe wrote <table> <value>` to the console, reachable by `/jtx-commit <value>` (replies `committed <value>` after the method returned) and `/jtx-fail <value>` (replies with the exception message `jtx-probe-failure`), plus `/jtx-read` replying `table-a=<value> table-b=<value>` (`none` for a missing row); (b) an ordinary probe, module name `JtxPlainProbe`, with one table and no `@Transactional`, reachable by `/jtx-plain-write <value>` and `/jtx-plain-read` (replies `plain=<value>`, `none` for a missing row); a Linux host where the server process can be killed with `kill -9` | 1. Start the server and wait for both probes to load. 2. Run `/jtx-commit v1`, then `/jtx-read`. 3. Run `/jtx-fail v2`, then `/jtx-read`. 4. Run `/jtx-commit v3`; as soon as its `committed v3` reply appears, kill the server process with `kill -9` (no clean stop). 5. List `plugins/UltiTools/data/JtxTxProbe/.ultitools-journal/` before restarting, start the server again and read its console, then run `/jtx-read`. 6. Run `/jtx-plain-write p1`, then `/jtx-plain-read`. 7. With the server stopped cleanly, run `sha256sum plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` again | 2: `committed v1`, then `table-a=v1 table-b=v1`. 3: the console shows `jtx-probe wrote jtx_a v2` and `jtx-probe wrote jtx_b v2`, the failing command's reply carries `jtx-probe-failure`, and `/jtx-read` still shows `table-a=v1 table-b=v1`, so neither table kept `v2`. 4: `committed v3` appears before the kill. 5: the listing holds at least one file whose name ends in `.commit` (a committed record not yet in the table files); the start-up console has no line of level ERROR or SEVERE that names `.ultitools-journal` or `JtxTxProbe`; `/jtx-read` shows `table-a=v3 table-b=v3`, the last committed values in both tables, and never `v1` in one table with `v3` in the other. 6: `/jtx-plain-read` shows `plain=p1`. 7: the SHA-256 equals the one recorded before step 1 | server | |

Cleanup: `/world set world pvp true` | 1: exactly one console line containing `Assigned an id to N row(s) of table 'world_settings' that had none` (N as counted in the Preconditions), and `/world info world` shows `PVP: Enabled`. 2: `world.set.success` for the set; after the restart `/world info world` shows `PVP: Disabled` — before 6.3.0 it showed `Enabled` again, because the update matched no row — and the second start's console has no `Assigned an id` line for `world_settings`. 3: `0` | server | |
