No shipped command reports `Query#delete()`'s return value to an operator, and the two cases that distinguish a removed-row count from a match count (a row removed by another writer between the read and the delete, a matched row with a null id) cannot be arranged on demand on a real server | — | Held by `QueryDeleteCountTest`: on the JSON backend and on the relational backend, `delete()` returns 2 for two removed rows and a re-query finds neither (`jsonReturnsRemovedCount`, `sqliteReturnsRemovedCount`); a matched row another writer removed first is not counted (`jsonDoesNotCountARowItDidNotRemove`, `sqliteDoesNotCountARowItDidNotRemove`); an operator whose delete removes nothing reports 0 (`foreignOperatorThatRemovesNothingCountsZero`); a matched null-id row throws `DataAccessException` naming the entity type and nothing is deleted (`jsonRefusesNullIdRow`, `refusesBeforeDeletingAnything`) | server | |
| ultitools.storage.restart-survival | A row written through a `DataOperator` while the server is up (any module command backed by `@Table`) | Stop the server completely, then start it again, then read the same row back through the same module command | The same value is returned after restart, in whichever backend `ultitools.storage.backend-select` is currently active | server | |
| ultitools.storage.transaction-failure-paths | not covered — unit-pinned here. A rollback or commit that fails on a live connection cannot be arranged on demand on a real server, and an `Error` inside a module's transaction is not something an operator can trigger. Accepted limitation (maintainer decision of 2026-10-07): documented and closed on the unit test under Expected, not run on a real server | — | Held by `TransactionFailurePathsTest` (SQLite operator over an H2 file and MySQL operator over H2, both in MySQL mode): a rollback that fails once leaves the written row unstored, gives the caller its own exception, never switches auto-commit on and closes the connection (`failedRollbackDoesNotCommit`); under HikariCP nothing is stored, no connection stays active and the next transaction commits only its own row (`failedRollbackUnderAPoolLeavesNothing`); a commit injected to fail before it reaches the database stores nothing and is not followed by another commit (`failedCommitDoesNotCommitLater`); held by `CommitOutcomeUnknownTest` (the same two operators, and the acknowledgement case also under a HikariCP pool of one): a commit the database applied, whose acknowledgement then fails, leaves the row stored and raises the same `TRANSACTION_FAILED` `DataAccessException` (same type, error code, message and cause type) as a commit refused before the database, where the row is absent, and in both cases exactly one commit is issued, auto-commit is not switched on afterwards and the connection is discarded, so the outcome of a failed SQLite or MySQL commit is unknown to the caller (`commitAcknowledgementLostLeavesTheRowStored`, `commitAcknowledgementLostUnderAPoolLeavesTheRowStored`, `commitRefusedBeforeTheDatabaseLeavesNothing`, `sameFailureEitherWay`, `noRetryAndNoAutoCommitAfterAFailedCommit`); a rollback-only commit whose rollback fails stores nothing and throws `UnexpectedRollbackException` (`rollbackOnlyWithAFailedRollbackStoresNothing`); an `Error` rolls back, is rethrown as the same instance on both overloads, leaves no context, and the next transaction starts at depth 1 (`ErrorOnRelational`); a throwing rollback is suppressed on the original (`rollbackFailureIsSuppressed`); the JSON engine discards the transaction's staged writes on an `Error` (`ErrorOnJson`); a failed id backfill whose rollback fails stores no partial assignment (`StartupIdBackfill`); controls without injected failures and `@Transactional` with an `Error` on H2 behave as before (`Controls`) | server | |
| ultitools.storage.where-comparisons | Checkout at the candidate head; JDK 21 and Maven dependencies available. The real-MySQL lanes run only against a throwaway local MySQL database whose name starts with `ultitools_it`, passed as the `ultitools.test.mysql.url` property (with `ultitools.test.mysql.user`) and the password in the `ULTITOOLS_TEST_MYSQL_PASSWORD` environment variable; without the property they are skipped, and a skipped lane is not evidence | Run `mvn test -Dtest='RelationalLiteralMatchTest,RelationalOrderingContractTest,RelationalExistenceCheckTest,SqliteFloatThresholdTest'`, then the first two again with `-Dultitools.test.mysql.url=jdbc:mysql://127.0.0.1:<port>/ultitools_it -Dultitools.test.mysql.user=root`; read the Surefire reports of each nested lane | All pass, and with the property every `RealMysql` lane reports 0 skipped. `RelationalLiteralMatchTest`, on real SQLite (the driver Paper bundles), `SQLiteDataOperator` and `MysqlDataOperator` over H2 in MySQL mode, real MySQL and JSON: `getAll`, `exist`, `page`, `del` and `updateIf` with INCLUDE, STARTSWITH or ENDSWITH select exactly the labels Java's `contains`, `startsWith` or `endsWith` selects, for values holding `%`, `_`, `!` and `\`, alone and back to back (`!!`, `!%`, `%_`) (`getAllMatchesLiterally`, `existMatchesLiterally`, `pageMatchesLiterally`, `delRemovesOnlyLiteralMatches`, `updateIfWritesOnlyLiteralMatches`); STARTSWITH `world_` neither reads nor deletes `worldX` and leaves the table definition unchanged (`startsWithUnderscoreIsLiteral`); `getLike`, `Query#like`, EQUAL and a null INCLUDE value keep their results (`unchangedOperatorsKeepTheirBehaviour`); INCLUDE `WORLD` matches the four lower-case `world` rows on real SQLite and real MySQL and none on H2 and JSON (`letterCaseIsTheBackendsOwn`). `RelationalOrderingContractTest`: `updateIf` with `level` GREATER 10 writes neither the row holding 2 nor the row holding 9, and `getAll` with `level` GREATER 9 returns only the row holding 10 (`updateIfHonoursNumericPrecondition`); eighteen GREATER and LESS conditions on `int`, `Long`, `double` and `Float` fields over negatives, fractions, 9007199254740992, 9007199254740993, 9223372036854775806 and the `Float` boundaries LESS 0.1f, GREATER 1.1f, LESS 3.3f and GREATER 20.2f, each against a row holding that same value (so none selects its boundary row, and GREATER 20.2f selects nothing), select exactly what Java's typed compare selects in `getAll`, `exist`, `page`, `del` and `updateIf` on real SQLite and both H2 lanes, and what a double-precision compare selects on real MySQL default columns (`getAllFollowsTheOracle`, `existFollowsTheOracle`, `pageFollowsTheOracle`, `delRemovesExactlyTheOracle`, `updateIfWritesExactlyTheOracle`); columns declared `BIGINT` and `DOUBLE` follow Java's typed compare on every lane, real MySQL included (`numericDeclaredColumnsUnchanged`); a String field still orders as text, EQUAL `level` 10 matches one row and EQUAL `stamp` 9007199254740993 matches one row (two on real MySQL text columns) (`unchangedOperatorsKeepTheirBehaviour`); a SQLite table created beforehand with TEXT columns keeps its `CREATE TABLE` text, ids and values, every numeric value stays `typeof` text, and `level` and `stamp` conditions follow Java's typed compare (`existingTextTableKeepsDefinitionAndCompares`); JSON with operator-assigned UUID ids selects what a double-precision compare selects (`jsonOrdersInDoublePrecision`); every numeric field has a condition a text comparison gets wrong, a `stamp` condition a double comparison gets wrong, and four `weight` conditions a 15-significant-digit reading of the stored float gets wrong, in both directions and one with no exact match (`everyFieldAndPrecisionIsExercised`); on real SQLite a `double` field written as 1/3.0 reads back as 0.333333333333333, `ratio` LESS 1/3.0 selects it, LESS and GREATER its read-back value select nothing, and `updateIf` with LESS its read-back value returns false (`doubleFieldComparesTheValueItReadsBack`). `RelationalExistenceCheckTest`: on real SQLite, `exist(WhereCondition...)`, `exist(entity)` and an `eq`-only `Query#exists()` return true for a stored match and false for none without throwing (`existCountsMatchingRowsOnRealSqlite`, `existByEntityOnRealSqlite`, `queryExistsOnRealSqlite`), as on H2 in MySQL mode (`existCountsMatchingRowsOnH2MysqlMode`, `existByEntityOnH2MysqlMode`, `queryExistsOnH2MysqlMode`). `SqliteFloatThresholdTest`, on real SQLite and `SQLiteDataOperator` over H2 in MySQL mode, each on a default and a `REAL`-declared `Float` column holding -2.5f, 0.0f, 0.1f, 1.1f, 3.3f, 20.2f, 2^60 and `Float.MAX_VALUE`: `del` with `weight` GREATER `Float` or `Double` +Infinity, `BigDecimal` 1E+400 or `BigInteger` 10^400 leaves all eight rows (`delGreaterThanEveryFloatDeletesNothing`); `updateIf` with `weight` LESS +Infinity applies to and writes all eight rows (`updateIfLessThanInfinityWrites`); `getAll` and `exist` with 25 thresholds of every number class (`Float`, `Double`, `Integer`, `Short`, `Byte`, `Long`, `BigDecimal`, `BigInteger`, infinities, out-of-range numbers, the `Float` boundaries) select exactly the rows an exact Java compare selects (`numberThresholdsFollowJava`); `weight` GREATER `Long` 2^60 selects only the `Float.MAX_VALUE` row and LESS a `BigDecimal` exactly equal to 0.1f selects only -2.5f and 0.0f (`valueEqualToStoredFloatIsNotSelected`); `weight` LESS `"abc"`, GREATER `"abc"`, LESS `""`, GREATER `"Infinity"`, LESS `"1e999"` and GREATER `"10"` select what `CAST(weight AS DOUBLE)` with the plainly bound text selects on the same database, or throw `DataAccessException` where that SQL fails (`textThresholdsSelectAsBefore`); on real SQLite `del` with LESS -Infinity or a NaN value leaves all eight rows (`nonFiniteThresholdsDeleteNothing`), `Double` thresholds 0.1, 1.1, ±1e300, `Double.MAX_VALUE` and `Double.MIN_VALUE` follow the exact Java compare (`finiteDoubleThresholdsFollowJava`), and GREATER `Math.nextDown((double) 0.1f)` does not select the 0.1f row on either column, the documented limitation (`seventeenDigitDoubleNextToStoredFloatComparesAsEqual`); on JSON, `getAll`, `exist`, `page`, `del` and `updateIf` with a `Float` or `Double` infinity or NaN under GREATER, LESS or EQUAL throw `IllegalArgumentException` and change no row (`nonFiniteValuesThrow`), and `updateIf` on an id that does not exist returns false (`updateIfOnMissingRowReturnsFalse`) | unit | |

## Module configuration persistence

Every row below that stops the server uses the console `stop` command and waits for the process to exit.
As of 6.3.0 nothing writes configuration at stop, module unload or module replacement (maintainer decision of
2026-10-04); a row proves it by comparing the file's `sha256sum` and `stat -c %Y` before and after the stop.
Killing the process, `/ul reload`, or a module's own reload command is a different path.
Later rows explicitly exercise other initialization, reload, panel, and interruption paths. "Disable line" means the console line
`[UltiTools] Disabling UltiTools v<version>` that Paper prints when it disables the framework; it
proves the framework's own stop sequence, which names never-saved module changes and writes nothing, actually ran.
Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.config.panel-map-entry` (the protocol v2 `config.update` edits only typed entries and
refuses a map leaf per key, `RemoteConfigEntries.java:7-14,442-444`, which `ultitools.config.panel-map-entry-server`
proves on the stub).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.config.shutdown-keeps-operator-edit | UltiCleaner loaded and enabled (its name in the startup log); `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/UltiCleaner/config/cleaner.yml` exists with `item.enabled: true` (shipped default); record the file's current `item.interval` as `<R>` (shipped default `300`); `<R>` is not `3000`, otherwise pick another edit value `<E>` in `10`–`3600` that differs from `<R>` and use it in place of `3000` below. UltiCleaner reads `item.interval` and never changes it in memory (its only uses are `getItemCleanInterval()` reads in `CleanerService`) | 1. From the console run `/clean status` and record the number `<S0>` from the line `Next item cleanup: N seconds` (N is the number; under `language: zh` the same line reads `下次物品清理: N 秒`). 2. With the server still running, change `item.interval` in `cleaner.yml` to `3000`, save the file, and record `sha256sum` of the file as `<H1>` and `stat -c %Y` as `<M1>`. 3. Run `/clean status` again and record `<S1>`. 4. Run `stop` and wait for the process to exit. 5. Record `sha256sum` of `cleaner.yml` as `<H2>`, `stat -c %Y` as `<M2>`, and read its `item.interval`. 6. Start the server and, within 60 seconds of the `Done` line, run `/clean status`; record `<S2>`. 7. Restore: set `item.interval` back to `<R>`, run `stop`, start the server, and confirm `/clean status` shows a number no greater than `<R>` | `<S0>` and `<S1>` are both no greater than `<R>` (the edit is not live before the restart, so the running value differs from the edited one); the stop console output contains the disable line and no WARNING line containing `cleaner.yml` (UltiCleaner changed nothing in memory, so nothing is named as never saved); `<H2>` equals `<H1>`, `<M2>` equals `<M1>` and `item.interval` still reads `3000` - nothing wrote the file at stop; `<S2>` is greater than `<R>` and no greater than `3000`. A `<H2>` different from `<H1>`, or `item.interval` back at `<R>`, is a fail (the pre-6.3.0 behaviour) | server | |

| ultitools.metrics.bstats-file-untouched | A disposable server backed up first, the UltiTools build under test installed; `plugins/bStats/config.yml` is valid and holds `serverUuid`. Server stopped: record the file's `sha256sum` and `stat -c %Y`. 2. Start the server and wait for `Done`. 3. Search the start-up log for `bStats`. 4. Record `sha256sum` and `stat -c %Y` again | Step 3: no UltiTools line saying bStats metrics are off for this run; step 4: the hash and modification time equal the recorded ones (UltiTools did not write the valid shared file). A changed hash with no other bStats user able to explain it, or an UltiTools metrics-off line, is a fail | server | |

| ultitools.config.registry-thread | Refactor checkout, JDK21 and Maven | Run `mvn test -Dtest=ConfigRegistryThreadConfinementTest,ConfigPanelThreadConfinementTest` | Nineteen tests pass: thirteen direct entries refuse before entity access, map snapshot cannot mutate/observe registry changes, three callbacks perform no operation/reply until their queued task runs, and protected update/upload callbacks return error rather than success after a manager refusal | unit | |
| ultitools.config.registry-release | Refactor checkout, JDK21 and Maven | Run `mvn test -Dtest=ConfigRegistryReleaseTest` | Sixteen tests pass: runtime unload removes entities without a later write; a runtime unload and an uninstall each name the never-saved key once, write nothing, and a released entity is not named again; a superseded owner is not named twice; throwing unload still removes them; close writes nothing before or after unload and a later report cannot write either; at shutdown a change made by the unload hook or by a container `@PreDestroy` is not written, also when the hook throws, a protected file survives, no entity's save is ever attempted while release still runs, and a full stop names the never-saved key once; runtime unload, runtime uninstall, failed activation and a superseded owner add no save | unit | |
| ultitools.config.main-thread-reload | Refactor checkout, JDK21 and Maven | Run `mvn test -Dtest=ConfigMainThreadGuardTest` | Six tests pass: four off-thread entries refuse with one warning and no changes; a held entity monitor cannot block the guard; no-server and server-thread reloads apply disk values | unit | |

## Configuration real-server obligations

Use the local `ConfigFixture` module from the Phase 17 UAT handoff, installed in the framework's
module directory (not Bukkit's top-level plugin directory). `F` below means
`plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/ConfigFixture/config/fixture.yml`. Record complete before/after
files and restore the per-row backup. Before the first row, start the server once with the fixture build under test
and stop it, so F holds every setting that build declares (a rebuilt fixture's first start appends any setting it
added, for example `# Map-valued entries` / `rules: {}` since plan 17-74); capture the baseline F only after that start.
A baseline captured before it makes every row that compares F across a start show those lines and give a wrong
verdict. Start from the positive fixture baseline for each row;
never carry a damaged file or ambiguous-map specimen into the next row. These server rows do not
count Maven tests as real-server evidence. No write normalizes layout: in every row, each byte of F outside what
the row's step writes stays as it was (compare with `diff` or `cmp`), and a file the write gate cannot write that way
is left unchanged with one WARNING naming the line to fix.

The fixture is the one plan 17-67 rebuilt: besides `capture`, `give`, `set <whole-key> <value>` (adds or changes
one `aliases` entry and calls `save()`), `show` and `saveloop <n>`, it has `setmem <value>` (changes `motto` in
memory only, reply `ConfigFixture motto in memory=<value> (not saved)`), `setmotto <value>` (changes `motto` and
calls `save()`, reply `ConfigFixture save() called with motto=<value>`; the reply does not report the write outcome -
F and the log are the evidence) and `opset <whole-key> <value>` (sets one `aliases` entry and writes exactly that
entry with `saveOperatorMapEntry`; reply `ConfigFixture operator change written: alias [<key>]=<value>`, or
`ConfigFixture operator change NOT saved: <reason>` with the in-memory change rolled back). Its baseline F has two
token entries, `enabled` and `mode`, whose framework comments are `# Fixture enabled (English token text).` and
`# Fixture mode (English token text).` under `language: en` (`# 测试配置启用（中文注释）。` and
`# 测试配置模式（中文注释）。` under `zh`); `show` prints `mode=<value>` on its line that starts `ConfigFixture price=`.
Plan 17-76 added the settings `warn-times`, `lines`, `rate` and `limits`, the command `decl-check <unchecked|plain>`, and
a last `show` line `ConfigFixture validation warn-times=<list> lines=<n> rate=<value> limits=<n>`. "F0" below is a copy of
the baseline F taken before the row.

## Language

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.language.select | `language: en` in config.yml, server restarted after the change | Run `/ul help`; separately, with the server running under `language: zh` and NOT freshly restarted, edit `language: "en"` and run `/ul reload` (no restart) instead, then run `/ul help` again | Both paths produce the English text `=== UltiTools Commands ===` block, not the Chinese equivalent — `UltiTools#reloadPlugins`'s `initLanguage()` call re-applies the language without a restart just as effectively as the restart path does, unlike `datasource.type` | server | |
| ultitools.language.select.neg-default | `language: zh` in config.yml (shipped default), server restarted after the change | Run `/ul help` | Output is the localized block from `lang/zh.json`'s help-text key, visibly different from the English `=== UltiTools Commands ===` block above, not a Chinese/English mix | server | |

| ultitools.language.file-preserve | Same base setup as `ultitools.language.file-refresh` (appears after it), except `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/<module>/lang/en.json` is hand-edited (any change, so its bytes no longer match the recorded provenance hash) BEFORE the rebuilt jar is staged, and then made read-only (`chmod a-w`) — since #608 an edited official file is restored at every start, so the per-key guard this row exercises applies only to an edited file the framework cannot restore; the rebuilt `<module>` jar's `lang/en.json` changes the `String.format` placeholder count of one existing key `<key>` relative to the currently-extracted version (e.g. `"Hi %s"` becoming `"Hi %s, you have %s items"`, adding a second `%s` parameter to a single-parameter message) — `<key>` is left untouched by the hand-edit above, isolating the arity check from the unrelated hand-edited key | Stop the server, replace the installed `<module>` jar with the rebuilt one, restart | `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/<module>/lang/en.json` on disk is byte-for-byte unchanged from the hand-edited version (confirm with a checksum taken before and after the restart) — the file is NOT overwritten, and no `lang/en.json.bak` exists; the startup console log contains a line whose text (module-name-derived console prefixing aside) is `[<module>] Language file '<path>/lang/en.json' for module '<module>' is not writable; treating it as operator-pinned and leaving it untouched instead of refreshing it from the bundled version.` (`<path>` the absolute path of `plugins/UltiTools/pluginConfig/<module>`), and a line `[<module>] Language key '<key>' in 'lang/en.json' for module '<module>' has a different placeholder count than the current bundled version; using the current bundled version's value for this key.` (the exact literal `PluginLogger.warn` argument this code path builds — `UltiToolsPlugin#applyPlaceholderArityOverride`); triggering `<key>`'s message afterwards (e.g. via the module's own command or event that renders it) shows the NEW jar's placeholder count (both `%s` parameters substituted), not the old single-placeholder form, while every OTHER hand-edited key in the same file still renders with the hand-edited text | server | |

## Panel capabilities

As of 6.3.1 every row below runs on the stub (protocol v2): it sets the named `ultipanel.capabilities.*` key,
then sends the matching request with `POST /_stub/send` or reads the matching outbound frames. The switches decide
the capabilities the server declares in its `hello` (mapping in `AgentCapabilities`' class comment: `commands`;
`files:read` and `config:read` from `file-read`; `files:write` and `config:write` from `file-write`; `files:delete`;
`logs`; `telemetry` from `monitoring`; `events:players` and `events:commands` from `player-events`;
`properties:read` and `properties:write` from `server-properties`). Every inbound request is checked again against
its switch when it arrives (`AgentProtocol#inboundAllowed`), so a request whose switch is off is answered `error`
`capability_not_accepted`, with `details.capability` the contract capability and `message` the sentence
`Capability#refusalMessage()` produces (`Blocked by policy — edit 'ultipanel.capabilities.<key>' in
plugins/UltiTools/config.yml to change this.`), and it adds one DENIED line to
`plugins/UltiTools/security/action.log.0` whose `action` is the request type and whose `target` is the request's
`command` or `path`, or else its type (`CloudFeatures#recordSwitchRefusal`). A `/ul reload` that changes the set of
declared capabilities closes the connection normally and reconnects with a new `hello` (`UltiCloud: the reload
changed which capabilities this server offers; reconnecting so UltiCloud sees the new set.`,
`ultitools.cloud.capability-switch-off`), so `/ul reload` is enough for every switch. A row's Preconditions name the
state of its switch and the newest `hello` (`GET /_stub/frames?type=hello`) that shows it. The stub sends a request
whatever the `hello` declared and notes `WS-NOTE … <type> sent although its capability …` when it was not declared.
Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.capability.file-delete.recursive` and `.neg-recursive-unset` (the contract's
`files.delete` has no `recursive` field and a directory is never deleted, see `.neg-directory`), and
`ultitools.capability.server-properties.update-config` and `.neg-update-config-disabled` (they sent the v1
`update_config` with `fileName: server_properties`; protocol v2 reaches `server.properties` only through
`properties.get` and `properties.update`, whose switch the two `server-properties` rows check with `file-write` set
the other way).

## Live log stream controls

As of 6.3.1 the log stream is protocol v2's `log.lines`: while `logs` is accepted the server sends the console's
lines every second (`CloudFeatures.java:103,265`), filtered by `ultipanel.logging.levels` and
`ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers` as read when the connection's log capture is attached
(`AgentOutboundFeeds.java:342-353`, `SystemLogHandler#loadConfiguration`); a `log.lines` entry carries only `ts`,
`level` (`DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN` or `ERROR`) and `message`. Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.remote.log-stream-pause`,
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-levels`, `ultitools.remote.log-stream-levels-direct`,
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-batch-interval` (with `.neg-below-floor`), `ultitools.remote.log-stream-start-stop` and
`ultitools.remote.log-stream-status`: each sent the v1 `log_stream` message, and the protocol v2 contract has no panel
request that controls or queries the log stream. The unit-pinned rows below hold the v1 batch sender, which 6.3.1
keeps, unreachable, until 6.4.0 (22-DECISIONS S-67).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.remote.error-auto-report | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; the UAT probe module (handover item 5) loaded; `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` and `ultipanel.logging.error-reporting.enabled: true` (shipped defaults); `ultipanel.logging.levels: [info, warning]` added to config.yml (it leaves out `error`), the server restarted after the edit; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected, its newest `hello` declaring `logs` and `errors`; a game player `P1` in game | 1. `P1` runs `/uatsecret fail uat-err`. 2. Ten seconds later `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines` and `GET /_stub/frames?type=error.report`. 3. Cleanup: remove `ultipanel.logging.levels` and restart | Step 2: one `error.report` frame whose payload `message` is `uatprobe failure on /uatsecret ***`, while no `log.lines` entry on that connection has `level` `ERROR` and none contains `uatprobe failure`; `log.lines` entries at level `INFO` keep arriving (the control): the `levels` setting filters only the log stream, never error reporting (`SystemLogHandler.java:230-238,247-256`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-wording | If extracting from the built JAR: the jar under `target/` is a fresh build of the commit being verified, not a stale artifact from an earlier build in the same worktree. If reading source directly instead: checked out at this pull request's head | Extract `config.yml` from the built JAR (`unzip -p target/UltiTools-API-<version>.jar config.yml`) or read `src/main/resources/config.yml` directly; locate the comment lines immediately above `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` and the comment lines immediately above `ultipanel.logging.batch.interval: 5000`; separately read `Capability.LOGS`'s description strings in `src/main/java/com/ultikits/ultitools/entities/Capability.java`; separately read the comment lines immediately above `logs: true` in `config-example.yml` at the repository root | The `logs` comment in `config.yml` (English and Chinese), the `logs` comment in `config-example.yml` (English and Chinese), and `Capability.LOGS`'s compiled description all state that the switch decides whether the live log stream is delivered ("Whether the panel receives the live console log stream." / "面板是否可以接收实时控制台日志流。"), with no reference to the panel controlling, pausing or resuming the stream — none of the three contains the English phrase "stream and control" or the Chinese phrase "接收并控制"; the `batch.interval` comment in `config.yml` (English and Chinese) states the applied value comes from the framework's currently loaded configuration each time a panel connection opens, not fixed once at server start; that this is the in-memory configuration rather than a direct disk read, so an on-disk edit needs `ul reload` before the next connection picks it up; and that restarting the server is the single dependable way to apply a changed value because `ul reload` alone does not reopen the panel connection — neither language contains a "read once"/"once, at server start" ("只在服务器启动时读取一次") claim (#495 fixed) | protocol | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-held-batch | not covered — unit-pinned. A send failing between the connection check and the write (the socket closing while a batch is in flight) cannot be arranged on demand on a real server | — | Held by `LogBatchHoldTest`: on the transmitter's own sender a throwing send and a send into an already-closed socket keep the batch and send it first (`throwingSendIsHeldAndSentFirst`, `closedSocketSendIsHeldAndSentAfterReconnect`), and no newer record is drained while it is held (`noNewerRecordIsDrainedWhileHeld`); the monitor's `batch_update` drain does the same (`throwingExternalSendIsHeld`, `closedSocketExternalSendIsHeld`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-overflow-report | not covered — unit-pinned. With the panel connected the queue is drained each time it reaches the batch size, and a disconnected server does not queue at all, so an overflow cannot be produced on demand | — | Held by `LogQueueOverflowReportTest`: 1005 records into a full queue report `discarded 5 record(s)` once, a second report within the interval is suppressed, the next interval reports the new count (`overflowDiscardsAreCountedAndReportedOncePerInterval`); nothing discarded, nothing reported (`noDiscardNoReport`) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-relog-safe | not covered — unit-pinned. The deadlock needs the socket to close between the drain thread's `isOpen()` check and `onClose`, while the main thread crosses the batch-size threshold at the same moment; neither can be arranged on demand on a real server | — | Held by `SysErrRelogDeadlockTest` (Paper's `SysoutCatcher` reproduced: a `System.err` line is re-logged through the plugin logger): the client's closed-socket diagnostic sent under the drain lock does not deadlock, reaches the console and is not streamed (`closedSocketDiagnosticUnderDrainLockDoesNotDeadlock`); any `System.err` line re-logged under the drain lock does not deadlock and is not streamed (`anyRelogUnderDrainLockDoesNotDeadlock`); control: the same line outside the drain lock is streamed (`relogWithoutDrainLockIsStillStreamed`). Both deadlock cases fail on `fd875537` | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-early-boot | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` (shipped default); `ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers` absent or not naming `Minecraft`; the stub server root linked to the stub | 1. Restart the server. 2. After `UltiCloud: connected.`, wait ten seconds; read the access log's `WS-OPEN` line of the new connection and `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines`. 3. Control: read the server's `logs/latest.log` | Step 2: a `log.lines` frame on the new connection carries an entry whose `message` is `[UltiTools-API] Plugin load order resolved successfully.` (logged while modules load, `PluginManager.java:3675`) and whose `ts` is earlier than that connection's `WS-OPEN` time: the start-up records are kept and replayed to the first connection, each with the time it was logged (`AgentOutboundFeeds.java:342-353`, `EarlyLogCapture#drainInto`). Step 3: `latest.log` holds the same line, earlier than `UltiCloud: connected.` (before 6.3.0 the line never reached the panel, #487) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-console-mirror | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.logs: true` and `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`; `ultipanel.logging.levels` and `ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers` absent (shipped defaults); `commands.log: true` in `spigot.yml` (the default); the server runs Paper's own Log4j configuration (no asynchronous loggers); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; an operator in game | 1. The operator leaves the server and joins again. 2. The operator runs `/list` in game. 3. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"command.execute","payload":{"command":"say mirror-check"}}`. 4. Read the server console for the lines steps 1-3 produced. 5. Five seconds later `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines` | Step 5: entries whose `message` is the console line's text without colour codes and, for a plugin's own line, without the `[<plugin>] ` prefix Paper adds when it prints that line on the console: for step 1 the operator's quit line and join line; for step 2 `<operator> issued server command: /list` (the operator's own `list` reply goes to that operator only, so it is neither on the console nor in the stream); for step 3 the `say` broadcast line and `[Remote command] > say mirror-check`, the plugin line appearing exactly once although Paper also copies it into Log4j (before 6.3.0 none of these console lines except that plugin line reached the panel) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.log-stream-mirror-forwarder-missing | not covered — unit-pinned here. Every supported Paper 1.21 build has the forwarder under exactly that name (checked on 1.20.6, 1.21.1, 1.21.4 and the 1.21.11 real server, #583), so the trigger cannot be reached on a supported server; accepted limitation (maintainer decision of 2026-10-07): documented and closed on the unit test under Expected, the real-server negative (no warning, the mirror installs) recorded as evidence | — | Held by `ConsoleMirrorForwarderMissingTest`: with a forwarder that forwards like Paper's but under another binary name, `install()` returns false, no `UltiToolsConsoleMirror` appender is on Log4j's root logger, one WARNING containing `will not mirror the server console` is logged, the forwarder is left as it was, and a plugin line reaches the transmitter once (`missingForwarderFailsClosed`); a second `install()` logs no second WARNING (`warnsOncePerRun`); with Paper's name the mirror installs and a plugin line arrives once (`presentForwarderInstallsAsBefore`) | server | |

## Panel upload and metrics honesty

Retired for 6.3.1: `ultitools.remote.upload-config` and `.neg-permissions` (the protocol v2 contract has no
`upload_config` message; configuration files are edited with `config.update`) and `ultitools.remote.disk-usage` (the
v2 `status` frame has no disk-usage field, `AgentTelemetry.java:388-397`, so the panel no longer receives it).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.remote.enabled-plugins | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.monitoring: true` (shipped default); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; a way to put one non-essential, already-loaded plugin `<target>` into the loaded-but-disabled state without removing it from `Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugins()` (for example `Bukkit.getPluginManager().disablePlugin(target)` from a console script), restored once the row is done | 1. `GET /_stub/frames?type=plugins.snapshot` and record the newest frame's `payload.plugins` as `<before>`. 2. Put `<target>` into the loaded-but-disabled state. 3. Twenty-five seconds later read the newest `plugins.snapshot` again | Step 3: the newest `plugins.snapshot` lists the same plugins as `<before>`, `<target>` included, and `<target>`'s entry now has `enabled` false while every other entry's `enabled` is unchanged (`AgentTelemetry.java:478-488`): the snapshot reports each plugin's real state, not every installed plugin as enabled (#437). A `plugins.snapshot` is sent at most once every 10 seconds when the list changed (`AgentTelemetry.java:57`) | server | |

## Remote surface guards

As of 6.3.1 every row below runs on the stub (protocol v2). A request path is a contract `ServerPath`, which never
holds a `.` or `..` segment, a leading `/`, a backslash or a colon: the stub answers such a `POST /_stub/send` 400
and sends nothing, so the framework's own handling of `..` after a symbolic link is reached by no protocol v2
request. A refused file request is answered `error` `path_not_allowed` with the guard's refusal sentence as
`message` (`RemoteFileOperations.java:667-673`), and a `files.list` leaves out every entry the guard would refuse
instead of marking it (`RemoteFileOperations.java:286-290`). Retired for 6.3.1:
`ultitools.remote.server-properties-safe-keys.neg-unsafe-key` (the contract's `properties.update` cannot name a key
outside its twelve, so no request reaches the framework; `ultitools.cloud.properties.update.neg-refused-key` proves
it for `online-mode` and `white-list`).

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.remote.command-blocklist | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`; `ultipanel.commands.blocklist` at its shipped default (it names `ban-ip`); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected | 1. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"command.execute","payload":{"command":"ban-ip 192.0.2.1"}}`. 2. `GET /_stub/frames?type=command.result`; read the console and the newest line of `plugins/UltiTools/security/action.log.0`; run `banlist ips` from the console | Step 2: after its `command.ack`, one `command.result` replying to the request with the payload `{"status":"error","code":"command_blocked","message":"command 'ban-ip' is on the remote command blocklist — Blocked by policy — edit 'ultipanel.commands.blocklist' in plugins/UltiTools/config.yml to change this.","details":{"matched":"ban-ip"}}`; the console holds the WARNING `[Remote command] Blocked: ban-ip 192.0.2.1`; the action-log line has `"capability":"COMMANDS"`, `"action":"execute_command"`, `"target":"ban-ip 192.0.2.1"`, `"verdict":"DENIED"` and a `reason` naming `ultipanel.commands.blocklist`; `banlist ips` does not list `192.0.2.1` (`CommandExecutionManager.java:269-294,359-370`) | server | |

| ultitools.remote.server-properties-safe-keys | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.server-properties: true`; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; copy `server.properties` aside | 1. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"properties.get","payload":{}}`; record `data.version` as `<V>`. 2. `POST /_stub/send {"type":"properties.update","payload":{"baseVersion":"<V>","changes":{"motd":"§a欢迎"}}}`. 3. Read the `properties.update.result`; `diff` the copy against the file. 4. `properties.get` again | Step 3: `status` `ok`, `data.keys` `[{"key":"motd","ok":true,"message":null}]`; `diff` shows exactly the `motd` line changed. Step 4: `data.values.motd` is `§a欢迎` (no mojibake) | server | |
| ultitools.remote.server-properties-safe-keys.neg-absent-on-server | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.server-properties: true`; a writable key that this server's own `server.properties` genuinely does not have — `pvp` on a Paper 1.21.11 first-start file (verify with `grep` against the real file; a key present with an empty value still counts as present); the stub server root linked to the stub and connected | 1. Record `sha256sum server.properties` as `<V>` and `POST /_stub/send {"type":"properties.update","payload":{"baseVersion":"<V>","changes":{"pvp":false}}}`. 2. Read the `properties.update.result`; run `sha256sum server.properties` | Step 2: `status` `partial` with `data.keys` `[{"key":"pvp","ok":false,"message":"server.properties does not define this key"}]` and `data.version` `<V>` (`ServerPropertiesManager.java:269-272`); the `sha256sum` is still `<V>`: nothing was added for a key the server does not read | server | |
| ultitools.remote.server-properties-one-line | Env: `stub`; `language: en` in config.yml; `ultipanel.capabilities.server-properties: true`; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; the server running (Paper re-writes `server.properties` itself at every start, so every edit below is made while it runs); back up `server.properties` | 1. Edit `server.properties` on disk: add the line `# operator note: keep this line` below Paper's two header comment lines and set `motd=§a欢迎 测试` (UTF-8); copy the file to P0. 2. `properties.get` for `<V>`, then `properties.update` with `{"max-players":<a value different from the current one>}`; `diff P0 server.properties` and `cmp -l P0 server.properties`. 3. Add a second line `view-distance=11` at the end of the file; copy it to P1; `properties.get` for `<V>`, then `properties.update` with `{"max-players":<another new value>,"view-distance":6}`; read the result; `diff P1 server.properties`. 4. Remove the added `view-distance=11` line, restart, wait for `Done` and the reconnect, send `properties.get` | Step 2: `status` `ok`; the diff is exactly the `max-players` line; the header lines, `# operator note: keep this line` and `motd=§a欢迎 测试` keep their bytes (every `cmp -l` difference lies inside the `max-players` line). Step 3: `status` `partial` with `data.keys` `[{"key":"max-players","ok":true,"message":null},{"key":"view-distance","ok":false,"message":"the key is defined on more than one line (lines <a>, <b>)"}]`, naming both line numbers and no value (`ServerPropertiesManager.java:274-279`); the diff is exactly the `max-players` line. Step 4: `data.values.motd` is `§a欢迎 测试` (Paper itself re-wrote the file at start and dropped the operator comment, as every Paper version does — not a fail) | server | |

## Configuration

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.config.batch-buffering | Checkout at the refactor head; JDK 21 and Maven dependencies available | Run `mvn test -Dtest=ConfigBatchBufferingTest`; inspect its refused-new-files, refused-existing-bytes, validation-before-write, outer-package-refusal, accepted-write-failure, protected-input and standalone controls | Seven tests pass: refused batches create/change no file; validation observes no pending file; an accepted second-file I/O failure preserves the first successful write and original second bytes, protects the failed entity and writes later files; standalone registration writes immediately | unit | |

One row per shipped yml file (D-06's config-per-file rule), not per key: `config.yml` (51 shipped leaf keys)
and `env.yml` (2 operator-relevant keys, `api-url` and `version`). Each row confirms every key in the file is present at
its `FEATURES.md`-documented default, then flips one representative key and observes the
behaviour follow.

| ID | Preconditions | Steps | Expected | Layer | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ultitools.config.config-yml | Env: `stub`; fresh `plugins/UltiTools/config.yml` initially at its shipped default (not hand-edited); `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true` required for the remote `say` after the shipped-default check below (shipped default: `false`); server started at least once; the shipped blocklist and editable-roots list keys are present; the UAT probe module (handover item 5) loaded; the stub server root linked to the stub and connected; a game player `P1` in game | Load the file; confirm each of the 51 shipped leaf keys documented under the companion `FEATURES.md` `## Configuration` section is present at its default, including the two list keys (`ultipanel.commands.blocklist`, `ultipanel.files.editable-roots`); then set `language: "en"` and `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: true`, restart the server, and run `/ul help`. Separately, in two configurations so neither filter masks the other (`ultipanel.logging.levels` and `excluded-loggers` are opt-in and absent from the shipped file): (a) set `ultipanel.logging.levels: ["error"]` only, restart, wait for `UltiCloud: connected.`, then `POST /_stub/send` a `command.execute` for `say levels-info` and let `P1` run `/uatsecret fail uat-err`; ten seconds later `GET /_stub/frames?type=log.lines`. (b) remove `levels`, set `ultipanel.logging.excluded-loggers: ["Minecraft"]`, restart, wait for `UltiCloud: connected.`; only then disable and re-enable the plain Bukkit plugin `UltiToolsExtExample` (`UltiTools-External-Example`, installed with a plugin manager able to do so without a restart), which makes `UltiToolsAPI#disconnect`/`#connect` log `[UltiTools-API] External plugin disconnected: UltiToolsExtExample` and `[UltiTools-API] External plugin registered: UltiToolsExtExample v<version>` through `Logger.getLogger("Minecraft")`; read `log.lines` ten seconds later; then remove the key again, restart and repeat the trigger. Cleanup: restore the shipped file, including `ultipanel.capabilities.commands: false` | Every one of the 51 shipped-resource leaf keys is present at its documented default, including both list keys; after the restart `/ul help` prints the English `=== UltiTools Commands ===` block. (a): an entry with `level` `ERROR` containing `uatprobe failure` arrives, and no entry has `level` `INFO` (none contains `levels-info`). (b): with the key set, neither external-plugin line appears in any `log.lines` entry while other entries keep arriving; with the key removed both appear: the exclusion acts on the record's real logger name (`SystemLogHandler.java:142-157`). The three `ultipanel.logging.batch.*` keys are shipped but are read only by the protocol v1 log transmitter, which 6.3.1 no longer runs: the v2 log stream sends `log.lines` every second whatever they hold (`CloudFeatures.java:103,265`), so they are not exercised here | protocol | |
| ultitools.config.env-yml | The jar handed over for this dispatch — a single clean build of the current commit at its default Maven properties, no `-Dultitools.api.url` override — is a fresh, unmodified extraction target, not a hand-edited copy; the `pom.xml` `<version>` at the built commit known in advance (read it before extracting). This is a build-time (Maven-filtered) property: it is exercised by the build that produced the handed-over jar, not by editing the shipped `env.yml` file after the fact, and this row proves only that default-property build | Unzip the handed-over jar and read `env.yml` | `env.yml` reads `api-url: "https://api.ultikits.com"` and `version: <the pom.xml version>` (e.g. `6.3.0`) — Maven's resource filtering resolved both placeholders, neither is the literal `${project.version}` token. This row proves only the default-property build; proving the `-Dultitools.api.url` override path requires a second, separately-built and separately-hashed jar handed over for that specific purpose — building one ad hoc during a dispatch is out of scope for this phase's boundary against constructing new product artifacts mid-run | protocol | |
